# RC-031 web disconnect/reconnect proof — locally verified bounded slice — 2026-10-03 (UTC)

- **Baseline and scope:** Repository baseline `7d45d657fd34198b49e31ceca22ffc26f477e486`; checkpoint committed as `917379f` and pushed to `origin/checkpoint/rc002-linux-runtime-evidence`. This checkpoint verifies the web disconnect/reconnect slice only; it does not change product source or RC-031 acceptance fields. The task remains In Progress and unaccepted. No deployment or release.
- **Evidence read, parent-verified this checkpoint:** Scratch `.../grok-goal-315708555552/implementer/run10/evidence.json` (source hashes match committed baseline; runner hash matches tree). Both viewports report 324,033 bytes produced after browser closure and before reopening, scrollback gap disclosed on resume, resumed producer marker, readiness 200, original session retained, different workspace hidden with input disabled. Mutation counts unchanged across reconnect bootstrap (one start, 15 inputs, one resize); persisted SQLite readback records the same terminal ID while running. Run10 reports result `web_terminal_disconnect_reconnect_renderer_resize_and_unknown_outcomes_passed`, final 6 terminals exited/removed, 0 sessions, integrity ok, all cleanup true, source unchanged. This accepts only the bounded reconnect slice; flow control, graceful live-actor shutdown, direct keyboard, and native surfaces remain open.
- **Advisor:** sam-claude-advisor consult attempted (model opus, effort high) via resolve_advisor.py; invocation failed with weekly Claude limit ("You've hit your weekly limit"). Recorded as unresolved/blocked, not as review evidence. Caller decision: proceed with checkpoint on direct evidence only, no advisor claims.
- **Reconnect boundary:** The browser context closes; after reopen, test explicitly restores the saved terminal reference into `sessionStorage` before page reload. This proves manual saved-reference restoration, not automatic product restoration. Test-only authenticated observer installs/uses its output gate before navigation; its host-side file write releases only test synchronization, not PTY input or a product action. The observer first checks that output is held, then polls while disconnected until more than 64 KiB and the end marker arrive. API readiness is checked throughout.
- **Unchanged valid evidence:** Earlier ANSI renderer and explicit-resize proof remains valid within its original scope; it is not repeated or upgraded by this checkpoint. Independent reviewer `01a10313` had identified timing and observer-window issues; the run10 reconnect record above is the corrected evidence, parent-verified as a bounded slice with the manual-restoration boundary stated. Mac API/client suite 163 pass/63 skip/0 fail/2050 assertions, typecheck, and docs checks passed in the parent handoff; these do not establish reconnect acceptance.
- **Publication attempt:** Account `@samfajreldines` verified via timeline snapshot; latest own post 30 Sep, over one hour old (interval eligible). Post click closed the mac-use bridge and disconnected the extension; no text entered or submitted. `browser_close` unverifiable while extension disconnected; owned tab physical removal unknown. Draft/outcome retained in scratch `x-check.json`. No new tab opened. Unavailable publication is not a skipped rate-limit interval.
- **Next:** Address flow control, then normal graceful shutdown with a live terminal. Direct keyboard input and native terminal surfaces remain open.

# RC-031 web ANSI renderer and explicit resize — locally verified bounded slice — 2026-10-03 (UTC)

- **Contract and scope:** Extend only web terminal rendering and explicit client resize; protected backend, native and file code remain unchanged from published code. One pinned MIT `@xterm/xterm@6.0.0` replaces plain-text output; no regex approximation, direct keyboard input or full-task approval. Readonly screen focus never sends keys. Columns and rows change only through an explicit Apply size action and a matching host acknowledgement. Resize uncertainty is persisted before one POST, and a readonly poll cannot clear it after a lost applied-response acknowledgement. Original RC-031 delivery, reconnect, flow-control, failure and dependency fields remain required, so RC-031 stays In Progress and unaccepted. Native terminal surfaces and direct keyboard input are not delivered.
- **Source and review:** Parent kept original 64KiB byte retention, serialized actual parser writes and resolves pending write callbacks on disposal. Reasoning reviewer `01a102b4-a1d6-7ed2-b7b1-8b6a77535999` found a real geometry-order defect: replay at 80×24 before accepting a larger host size clamps cursor placement. Parent applies validated poll dimensions synchronously before reset/replay; closure `01a102c5-dfba-77d2-9ba4-2f9b1cb63a33` found no remaining supported defect. Host metadata identified both as `chatgpt/gpt-6.1-sol`; they ran no tests. Independent code reviewer `01a102ee-1365-7f50-a1be-103b7d98440b` read the latest runner fixes and reported no material findings. Source hashes at proof time: `apps/web/src` `96b28ace…`, `packages/client/src` `b23280a6…`, `apps/api/src` `23cbffe7…` (unchanged from the published backend), runner `7123f1c2…`.
- **Component evidence:** Nine actual Chromium component cases cover CR overwrite, 120×40 row35/column100, SGR/alternate screen, split UTF8, pending write disposal, readonly keyboard focus and desktop/mobile local overflow. Fixture buttons call the shipped wrapper's public handle; this is component evidence, not a fake PTY or a user journey. The browser regression count rose from 88 to 97 passes, consistent with these nine cases.
- **Final real journeys:** Two consecutive runs of the unchanged source (proof6 and proof7) each exit 0 with result `web_terminal_renderer_resize_and_unknown_outcomes_passed`. Each uses normal passphrase login on the actual pinned Linux ARM64 TLS API, image `sha256:87416c977a612a204eb54ab9f3927023c2a3c971f4f345a01da08ea6262ae30e`, a unique named volume and Chromium 153.0.8010.12, at desktop 1440×1000 and mobile 390×844. Each viewport records CR overwrite, one SGR red span, alternate-screen restore, cursor placement, split UTF8, and a confirmed resize read back by `stty` inside the shell (120×40 desktop, 40×18 mobile) with host state `applied`. Falsifier: a real resize POST returns 200 upstream and the browser drops the response; the persisted resize-uncertain flag is read before the POST, input and further resize stay blocked, and readonly polling does not clear it. Another login receives 404 for the original terminal. No horizontal overflow (page scroll width equals viewport width). Each run ends with six terminals exited and removed, zero sessions, SQLite `quick_check` ok, source hashes unchanged, and browser, Vite, API and volume cleanup all true.
- **Negative proof:** A scratch-only copy of the runner replaced delivery of a late 200 with a rejected delivery. It exited 1 with result `unverified` and error `Late 200 cleared or contradicted durable uncertainty`, so the runner does not pass when late-response delivery fails; the uncertainty fence remained set. Its API exit left one running terminal pending cleanup, and the owned reconcile step moved it to exited and removed before volume removal; browser, Vite, API and volume cleanup all true. This proves the verdict guard, not task acceptance.
- **Retained failures (not passes):** proof1 failed a wrong assertion that rejected early visibility of the persisted uncertainty fence, which intentionally exists before the POST; the late-acknowledgement check must run after the timeout. Its API exit left a third actor closing/pending and the runner correctly kept the owned API container, volume and TLS. Recovery used the shipped `stopAll` inside an owned controller on the same volume, and readback shows three terminals exited/removed and SQLite `quick_check` ok. proof2 failed a host-state assertion, attributed to an Apply click ignored while a state read was in flight. proof3 failed on a click timeout, attributed to a file listed only after the first refresh. Both were runner corrections. proof4 and proof5 passed on an earlier runner revision and are superseded by proof6/7 on the final runner. The earlier historical scratch directory is unavailable; these captures are separate and are not durable files.
- **Regression and checks:** Mac API/client log 163 pass, 63 platform/configuration skips, 0 fail, 2050 assertions. Browser regression 97 pass, 3 environment skips. Typecheck and `python3 scripts/check-doc-links.py` exit 0. Shared-validator tests passed earlier (8 tests/288 assertions). These are separate from the real Linux journeys.
- **Not verified:** Native terminal UI, direct keyboard input, flow control, and disconnect/reconnect of a live producing process. A normal backend graceful shutdown that stops a live actor is unverified: runs that ended with a live actor relied on the runner's owned reconcile step. Source is published at commit `892cc45d1c5796576667163936470cb4305effaf`, confirmed by `git ls-remote`. GitHub runs 37143146756 and 37143148841 were both read back completed/success for that SHA; these configured CI checks are distinct from the real Linux journeys. No deployment, release or X publication is claimed. The original RC-002 Distill effect stays unknown with no retry, and RC-004 stays gated on RC-002 acceptance.
- **Next:** From the RC-031 contract, prove web disconnect and reconnect: a process prints more than the scrollback limit while the browser context closes and reopens, the same terminal resumes with a disclosed gap, API readiness stays healthy, and a second login and workspace never see or send input to it. Then address flow control, graceful live-actor shutdown, direct keyboard and native surfaces as separate slices. Do not repeat the ANSI/resize proofs.

# RC-031 protected terminal backend — local proof only — 2026-10-03 (UTC)

- **Contract and scope:** Preserve RC-031 Start, Delivery, Executable proof, Failure if and its accepted RC-028 dependency. - **Runtime and pivot:** Actual Linux ARM64/Bun1.3.13 image `sha256:87416c977a612a204eb54ab9f3927023c2a3c971f4f345a01da08ea6262ae30e`, Docker29.4.0 APIv1.47 under macOS-hosted Docker. Backend alone holds trusted private-host Docker control. Direct shells run UID/GID65534 in temporary constrained process containers with only `workspaces/<workspaceId>` mounted, no network/backend database/socket/environment, read-only rootfs, private PID/IPC and dropped capabilities. This revises one-physical-container execution, not the persistent backend or sole main-Distill agent harness. It is not shared-daemon or production-host isolation proof.
- **Filesystem foundation:** Optional identity applies only to new folders; nullable legacy identity remains current-process-owned, not bulk migrated. Held descriptor/device/inode/marker/mode checks stay binding through folder recovery and CREATE/OPEN/SAVE/MOVE. Linux identity2/3 passed212tests/one platform skip/2500assertions each. Parent rechecked all three ownership-source hashes unchanged. UID/GID `0xffffffff` failed a real Linux baseline because fchown treats it as unchanged; validation now rejects before reservation and persisted sentinel rows fail readiness. Mac validation covers malformed and incomplete schema identities.
- **Final executable result:** Backend8 and backend9 each run shipped `terminals.test.ts` plus `terminal-lifecycle.test.ts`:4pass/0fail/132assertions. Two genuine same-user logins deny foreign-login receipt/poll/input/resize/stop with404; duplicate confirmed start is canonical and changed-body conflicts. Real shell UID/cwd/initial80x24 and resized100x35, sequenced input/dedup/gap, backend environment/database/token/socket denial, CLI-file visibility through FileAPI, output ring overflow/health and natural exit7 are inspected. Logout, genuinely elapsed4000ms session expiry and workspace archive deny further input (401/401/409) with unchanged sequence1. Lifecycle first writes `ready.txt` and reads exact bytes at the real mounted leaf, leaving an interactive shell ready to consume input, rather than keeping it in an infinite loop.
- **Authoritative readback and cleanup:** Each final run has4durable terminals `state=exited`, `cleanup=removed`; lifecycle records start/stop/remove sent1, natural-exit record start1/stop0/remove1. All4databases have sessions0 and `PRAGMA quick_check=ok`. Readback precedes removal. - **Reproduce:** Run `bun test apps/api/src/features/terminals.test.ts apps/api/src/features/terminal-lifecycle.test.ts` inside an actual root Linux host with Bun1.3.13, native `/var/run/docker.sock`, frozen Linux-compatible installed dependencies and a unique task-owned named data volume mounted at `/var/lib/remotecode`. Set `DATABASE_PATH=/var/lib/remotecode/default.sqlite`, `RC031_PROOF_DATA_ROOT=/var/lib/remotecode`, `RC031_PROOF_VOLUME=<that exact named volume>`, `RC031_PROOF_IMAGE=<approved digest>` and `RC031_PROOF_API_CONTAINER=<that exact host name>` for the backend environment only. - **SAVE concurrency ceiling:** Backend writers serialize through SQLite and compare exact byte versions. Linux pathname exchange does not offer hash-based compare-and-swap against arbitrary uncoordinated native writers. A final pre-exchange check reduces the observed sync window; a failed post-exchange witness must stay unknown and retain original/replacement inodes for reconciliation, never delete the displaced edit or repeat exchange. No atomic exclusion of all CLI/agent writes is claimed. MOVE, actual editing clients and real Distill approvals remain open.

- **Published CREATE protocol:** Owner-scoped POST `/api/workspaces/:workspaceId/files` and GET `/files/receipts/:requestId` normalize UUID aliases, reserve durable pending intent before filesystem work, prepare a private 0600 stage with exact UTF-8 bytes, and publish the verified open inode using `linkat` through `/proc/self/fd` without replacing a destination. FULL SQLite sync, file/directory sync, prepared identity/hash and immutable outcome distinguish accepted history from live files. Stage names stay hidden/reserved; recovery only witnesses a matching published regular file under a writer transaction and never republishes it. A retained stage after an interrupted publication remains untouched during receipt recovery. This is a direct-user API, not Distill approval proof.
- **Review repairs and falsifiers:** Initial source reviewer `01a0f72f-ea25-7f70-bd99-365a89dc97cd` found five source-supported defects: mutable stage pathname, surviving outcome with lost intent, insufficient receipt binding, unbounded recovery reads, uppercase UUID rejection. Worker repaired them and its focused Linux runs passed twice (18 pass/one skip/195 assertions). Parent then found swallowed recovery SQL errors committing completed intent without outcome, missing prepared input binding, and missing recovery sync. - **Scope and acceptance:** This is one browser consumer slice within RC-018. The original Start, Delivery / end, Executable proof, Failure if, and Depends on fields are unchanged. RC-018 remains In Progress; direct prerequisites RC-006, RC-008, RC-013, RC-004, and RC-016 are accepted.
- **Implementation:** A valid Sign in tap starts one 13-second deadline before `/api/version` capability negotiation and request-ID persistence. Capability lookup, storage readback and safe cleanup of an unsent identity, keyed POST, matching-cookie session check, and up to two read-only receipt lookups are bounded by the remaining time. An immediate preflight network/error response or preflight expiry sends no login and leaves no pending identity. If the login POST may have been sent at expiry, the request ID remains pending and the UI stays unknown; no POST is replayed. A historical receipt without the matching current cookie never authenticates the browser.
- **Red baseline:** `CMD-903` used the exact old web app from commit `dcbd1d8757c00f7f8c73ec926105a090fed94f18` in an isolated source copy. Both desktop and mobile preflight tests failed at the expected deadline-message assertion because the old client returned earlier on its independent request timeout. This failure is the intended timing-contract red result, not a successful product outcome. Each private SQLite readback had zero actions, mappings, sessions, and auth receipts; `PRAGMA quick_check` returned `ok`.
- **Focused current-source proof:** `CMD-904` was stable PASS with two runs, each exercising three real Chromium flows at desktop 1280×900 and mobile 390×844. The slow capability response expired without a login POST or identity; releasing it late still started no login, and a user retry created one login then logged out. Immediate capability transport failure showed “The host could not be checked. No login request was sent. Try again when the host is reachable.” with no POST, pending identity, cookie, or session. A login POST held before API acceptance timed out under the same tap-relative deadline; two automatic receipt reads stayed bounded, the pending ID remained unknown, and there was no replay. The user's explicit receipt check got the real API's 404 `receipt_not_found`, targeted revocation fenced that ID, and a distinct login/logout then succeeded.
- **Focused authoritative readback:** Each run used loopback Vite and the real Elysia API with a fresh private SQLite database. Both had zero actions, zero action mappings, zero sessions, two `login/closed_before_acceptance` receipts, four `login/session_created`, four `logout/sessions_revoked`, two `revoke_login/login_revoked`, and `quick_check=ok`. The final auth recovery spec contains 46 Playwright tests; the pre-slice inventory contained 40, and the three new scenarios are instantiated at both desktop and mobile sizes.
- **E2E impact audit/report:** Matched local baseline/final bundles have fingerprint `6f7ff8789e7080163bb43ed3ecc5f7db884162107dfc7e1403a24af988334e18` and `54d1cf1a12f0d557fb0e3efb94696fa18fbb01b4bf8b32ba3123d31315dfae3a`, respectively, at the same pre-slice base/head. `CMD-912` test-diff audit passed with zero issues; `CMD-913` report validation passed with decision COMPLETE for this bounded E2E slice only. The report records the 40-to-46 test wiring, desktop/mobile scenarios, local behavior proof, and cleanup. No screenshot or video was requested or created.
- **Broader regression evidence:** `CMD-905` passed all 46 auth recovery browser tests twice. Each full-suite SQLite database had zero actions/mappings, six remaining test sessions from auth scenarios, and `quick_check=ok`. `CMD-902` typecheck passed. The focused shared-client version-header test passed `CMD-906` three times; `CMD-907` passed the API/client suite twice (100 tests / 695 assertions each). An earlier repeated unit run (`CMD-936`) produced one unrelated 404 in that version-header assertion; its cause is unconfirmed, and later focused/full runs were stable.
- **Test corrections:** An attempted authenticated test-control route was not proxied by Vite and failed at setup with 404; that test-only change was reverted. The final proof uses browser request interception for controlled failures and real API/SQLite observations for recovery. An intermediate assertion expected a transient status instead of the final “no receipt” unknown state; it was corrected to assert only the allowed terminal unknown messages and the actual API `receipt_not_found` response. The final focused repeated run passed.
- **Environment and source identity:** macOS 26.6.2 arm64, Bun 1.3.13, Node 22.23.2, Playwright 1.63.0, and Playwright Chromium. The app source SHA-256 is `1294bb600c3bd3331f84e86ad6673eaa46db7c1535b7d3a02aa659e1138b8485`; the E2E spec SHA-256 is `bde9baab1a3360aa27de26f5e708ccc5fd64a187d9811f56fff3b8481cb580d5`. Test API and Vite bound to `127.0.0.1:37117` and `127.0.0.1:37118`; every run used a fresh SQLite database. Test-owned API/web processes were stopped after each run. The full-suite database retained six auth test sessions for its readback; it had no action rows/mappings and `quick_check=ok`.
- **X cadence and cleanup:** `mac-use__browser_status` returned `connected=true, hasTab=false`. The earlier agent-owned tab's physical removal remains unverified, so I did not open a replacement tab or inspect the account/latest own post. No X post was submitted; the one-hour cadence could not be verified safely.
- **Limits:** This is local browser proof, not deployed behavior or RC-018 acceptance. Android, ID-less callers, other mutation families, server/gateway deadlines, shared retry budgets, device-storage failure cleanup, and stale-generation transitions remain open. RC-002 remains Blocked with its provider effect unknown and untouched. No release, deployment, or phase advancement is claimed.

# RC-018 native keyed-login total deadline — partial — 2026-09-29

- **Contract and eligibility:** This is one native sign-in consumer slice within RC-018. The ticket's Start, Delivery / end, Executable proof, Failure if, and Depends on fields remain unchanged. RC-018's direct prerequisites RC-006, RC-008, RC-013, RC-004, and RC-016 are Complete; aggregate RC-017 acceptance remains open and is not an implementation prerequisite. RC-018 remains In Progress.
- **Behavior under test:** The native sign-in's existing 13-second observation budget now starts at the Sign in tap. Capability negotiation, request identity persistence, the one login POST, session confirmation, and the allowed receipt lookup consume that same budget. If the deadline expires after a POST may have been sent, the app retains its identity, reports an unknown result, disables another login, and never replays. An unsent request is handled separately; that branch remains the next slice.
- **Baseline setup correction:** An initial XCUITest used hard-coded `127.0.0.1:39211` while its isolated API was on another port; that attempt failed before reaching the behavior and is discarded. The final test derives its API origin from the installed app's visible `Host:` label. The valid red run `CMD-019` used the exact pre-fix `apps/mobile/App.tsx` from commit `4cc64693d2c1a563e4b5b76cca5a7ff8f5733c1f` (SHA-256 `04674806c3682c296cc83099ff781318b5fbf1112d123878e927e734682104de`). It failed at the tap-deadline assertion with one real keyed login POST pending before acceptance (`posts=1`, `accepted=0`, `receipts=0`, `receiptExists=0`). Its isolated SQLite returned `quick_check=ok`, had no auth receipt, and retained one observer session. The expected failing test is red evidence, not a passing product result.
- **Current-app installed proof:** `CMD-020` is a stable two-run pass on the task-owned iPhone 16 Pro / iOS 18.5 simulator, macOS 26.6.2, using the installed React Native app and a fresh loopback Elysia/SQLite API per run. App source SHA-256: `a874ab1c8c51c1f36c53655e39188cea82af42bbd13dc503e08ca3385660ced3`. Each run delayed `/api/version` for nine seconds, then held the actual keyed login before acceptance. At the 13-second tap-relative deadline, the UI stayed unknown with the request pending and Sign in disabled. After the fixture released the POST and returned its pre-acceptance rejection, the app did not authenticate or replay. The user-visible manual receipt lookup and targeted revocation fenced the old ID; a distinct login then succeeded and logout completed. Read-only SQLite checks for both runs returned `quick_check=ok`, zero actions, and zero final sessions; auth receipt rows were exactly two `login` receipts (one closed before acceptance and one session-created), one `revoke_login/login_revoked`, and one `logout/sessions_revoked`. The isolated worktree was restored byte-identically and task-owned API ports were released.
- **Fixture and local checks:** The native API fixture test suite `CMD-016` passed three times. Typecheck `CMD-017` passed. Full `bun run test` receipt `CMD-018` passed twice, 99 tests / 678 assertions each. The test-impact builder initially refused a temporary draft because a long synthetic password literal matched its secret-like-content scanner. The builder CLI help lists `--repo`, optional `--base`, `--head`, and repeated `--path`, environment kind/id, and `--out`, which writes `bundle.json` and `bundle.patch` outside the repository; `audit_test_diff.py` takes one bundle JSON path. The final XCTest reuses the existing class fixture credential; no assertion or task criterion was weakened. Before these final handoff edits, implementation/test bundle `final-v4` reported seven changed files and one test file; its audit returned `PASS`, `issues: []`, and no unsupported test files. The complete checkpoint bundle is rebuilt after the handoff edits. The first refused builder output identified a newly added long hard-coded fixture credential; it was synthetic test data, and the final source no longer includes that line. Source logs, receipts, bundle, audit, `.xcresult` bundles, databases, and per-run readbacks are under `{SCRATCH}/rc018-keyed-login-coverage.7jC8mb/` and `{SCRATCH}/rc018-login-deadline-target-runs/`.
- **Independent reasoning review:** Full-diff reviewer `01a0ee37-448c-7c02-b69e-2d08e80c0270` found no code, fixture, XCUITest, runner, or SQLite assertion defect, but caught a stale sentence in `plan/failure-state-contract.md` implying no installed-native login observation had been proved. Follow-up `01a0ee39-b4b3-70c2-b69e-2d08e80c0270` confirmed the native wording correction and caught a neighboring browser-login retry-count mismatch (one vs. up to two); follow-up `01a0ee3a-c276-7993-af62-422d40fa488c` confirmed the retry-count correction and no remaining contradiction in those records. Reviewers did not rerun tests or independently inspect the raw `.xcresult`/SQLite artifacts; receipts and readbacks above are from the parent-run checks. No RC-018 acceptance field changed.
- **Publication and CI:** Commit `bde3eb0cd5ca5282fa2d481191a90202e54b9101` (`fix(mobile): bound native login from sign-in tap`) was pushed to `origin/checkpoint/rc002-linux-runtime-evidence` and read back at the same SHA. GitHub Actions push run `36607729995` and pull-request run `36607737942` both passed `static` and `test` for that exact SHA. CI does not accept RC-018.
- **X and cleanup:** The verified account was `@samfajreldines`; its latest own post before this update was dated 27 Sep, more than one hour earlier. Published and read back the profile post: `RemoteCode's iOS sign-in now starts its 13-second deadline at the tap. Two simulator runs kept the pending login unknown after the deadline, with no replay. Explicit receipt/revocation recovery allowed a fresh login. Local proof, not deployed. #buildingRemoteCode`; it appeared 24 seconds old, and read-only public profile readback confirmed canonical URL `https://x.com/samfajreldines/status/2104993125592612962` with the full text. `browser_close` returned `closed:false`/`released:true`, and `browser_status` returned `hasTab:false`. Physical tab removal remains unverified; no replacement tab was opened.

# RC-018 iOS delayed action acceptance after the tap deadline — partial — 2026-09-29

- **Contract and scope:** This is one installed-iOS failure path within RC-018. The original Start, Delivery/end, Executable proof, Failure if, and Depends on fields are unchanged, and the five direct prerequisites remain Complete. No shipped `App.tsx` behavior changed in this test checkpoint; the tap-relative implementation is the published one from `ed6f1a9c098620b90e6dfb5594a8d562115a85e2` (app source SHA-256 `04674806c3682c296cc83099ff781318b5fbf1112d123878e927e734682104de`).
- **Test-design pivot:** Preflight-only draft `CMD-014` delayed `/api/version` past ten seconds, then expected no POST. It passed against old app source because that shared-client read already had its own ten-second timeout; SQLite showed no action or mapping and `quick_check=ok`. It was non-discriminating and was discarded. The final scenario delays capability negotiation by six seconds, then holds the real keyed action POST for five seconds in the API fixture's `onBeforeHandle`, before the Elysia action handler can accept it. The prior implementation's observation timer began only after request-ID persistence, so its separate POST cap left enough time for the late server response; the tap-relative implementation expires first.
- **Red baseline:** An isolated native XCUITest replaced only `apps/mobile/App.tsx` with the exact pre-fix source from commit `540f77d1fe5567fe9a6330e9fc4087000c44b310` (SHA-256 `30f2db9460ec01ae1dfa51e054b7f5162497f74f77a97f4e54305fc761b39be6`). `testActionPostAcceptedAfterTapDeadlineStaysUnknownUntilManualReceipt` failed at the tap-deadline assertion, not during setup. Its failure text recorded `posts=1`, `accepted=false`, `receipts=0`, and `matchingActions=0`: the real POST was held before acceptance, with no automatic receipt read or action row. SQLite readback returned `quick_check=ok` and zero actions/mappings. Two isolated sign-in sessions remained because the expected red assertion ended the test before sign-out. This is valid red evidence for the old deadline behavior; no action was accepted or counted as successful.
- **Installed-app target:** The final current-app XCUITest passed 1/1 on the task-owned iPhone 16 Pro / iOS 18.5 simulator (`5ACA224D-A727-471F-9917-E3599131515E`), built on macOS 26.6.2. The app source SHA-256 was `04674806c3682c296cc83099ff781318b5fbf1112d123878e927e734682104de`. The fixture delayed capability negotiation for six seconds, then held the real action POST before acceptance. By the tap-relative deadline the UI remained unknown and Submit stayed disabled; diagnostics showed one pending POST, zero receipt reads, and no matching action in history. The real API accepted one action at 11.5 seconds after the tap; the late success did not clear unknown or trigger a replay. Manual receipt lookup then confirmed the exact canonical action. The `.xcresult` reports 1 passed / 0 failed; SQLite readback found one action, one matching `action_requests` mapping, `quick_check=ok`, and zero sessions. The isolated worktree status was identical before and after, and API port 61531 was released.
- **Simulator restoration:** After the native runs, both protected simulators `A53A0CDA-5A7A-4D80-9009-CA024B374361` and `5ACA224D-A727-471F-9917-E3599131515E` showed `Shutdown`. Neither was erased or reset. Both were booted with `simctl`, each reached `Finished`, and the final device list confirmed both `Booted`.
- **Canonical response proof:** Reviewer inspection found that the fixture's delayed `onAfterHandle` branch could replace the real POST receipt with the request payload. The fixture now preserves the route's actual response. Integration test `CMD-037` sends the delayed keyed POST to the real Elysia/SQLite fixture and compares its 201 response body to canonical receipt lookup and action history; it passed twice. Typecheck `CMD-036` passed twice after explicit Eden-response union guards.
- **Recovery runner:** The first recovery-mode attempt `CMD-038` passed seven UI tests but failed its fixed SQLite assertion with four actions because the prior `testActionDeadlineStartsAtTapAndIgnoresLateReceiptWithoutReplay` was still selected. The recovery mode now skips both action-deadline tests. Corrected receipt `CMD-040` is a stable two-run pass: six selected XCUITests passed each time; each database contained exactly three expected actions, two request mappings, 27 auth receipts, zero sessions, and `quick_check=ok`. Parent independently read both result summaries and databases. Discovery `CMD-017` includes the new delayed-POST test; runner syntax receipt `CMD-041` passed twice.
- **Other iteration outcomes:** The direct API fixture test initially exceeded Bun's default five-second test timeout because the fixture intentionally delays acceptance for five seconds. Its test-specific limit is now eight seconds and it passes twice. A first typecheck found that Bun's `expect()` assertion did not narrow an Eden response union; explicit guards fixed it. One overlapping native-runner invocation was refused by its port guard; it did not start an API or test and is not used as evidence. The prior test-impact builder had twice returned “target contains no changed files”; it was not retried and no generated impact report is claimed.
- **Review race and correction:** Reasoning reviewer `01a0ed19-a03b-7740-ba98-d863f3a831a2` found that the earlier fixed-delay request could be accepted between the UI test's diagnostics and history reads. # RC-018 iOS action tap-to-result deadline — partial — 2026-09-29

- **Contract and eligibility:** This is one installed-iOS action path within RC-018. The original Start, Delivery/end, Executable proof, Failure if, and Depends on fields are unchanged; direct prerequisites RC-006, RC-008, RC-013, RC-004, and RC-016 remain Complete. RC-018 remains In Progress.
- **Behavior:** The iOS action's 10,000 ms observation deadline now starts at `submitAction` entry, before `GET /api/version` capability negotiation and request-ID persistence. Capability lookup, AsyncStorage identity preparation, the keyed POST (capped at six seconds), and receipt reads are bounded by the remaining budget. If time expires before sending, the UI reports that no action was sent and clears the unsent identity safely; if the POST may have committed, the UI remains unknown with its identity pending. A receipt read completing late cannot confirm after expiry, and no action POST is replayed. Manual receipt lookup can reconcile the eventual canonical result.
- **Red baseline:** Native XCUITest `testActionDeadlineStartsAtTapAndIgnoresLateReceiptWithoutReplay` failed against the exact pre-fix `apps/mobile/App.tsx` from commit `540f77d1fe5567fe9a6330e9fc4087000c44b310` (SHA-256 `30f2db9460ec01ae1dfa51e054b7f5162497f74f77a97f4e54305fc761b39be6`). The test did not observe the expected unknown state within its 12-second tap-relative limit. Read-only SQLite inspection confirmed the action had committed once with one request mapping; `quick_check` was `ok`. The isolated baseline database retained two sign-in sessions because this expected failure exited before the test's sign-out path.
- **Installed-app proof:** `CMD-007` is a stable two-run pass (`[0,0]`) on the task-owned iPhone 16 Pro / iOS 18.5 simulator (`5ACA224D-A727-471F-9917-E3599131515E`), built on macOS 26.6.2. Each real UI/API/SQLite journey delayed capability negotiation by six seconds, committed one action with a malformed response body, and delayed its first canonical receipt read past the tap-relative deadline. The UI reached unknown within the test's 12-second allowance, kept Submit disabled after the late receipt, then confirmed the same action only after a manual receipt check. Each run asserted one POST, one automatic receipt read, and a second read only after manual recovery. Independent SQLite readback found one action, one matching mapping, `quick_check=ok`, and zero remaining sessions for each run. The task-owned worktree's before/after status files were byte-identical for both runs; the root ignored `apps/mobile/ios`, pre-existing simulator, and untracked `scripts/__pycache__/` were preserved. ## RC-018 web action click-to-result budget — partial — 2026-09-29

- **Eligibility/scope:** Pre-slice `HEAD` and origin branch were `216bc65ae15c7990845ce157990ae3687e1d02d2`. RC-018's Start and accepted direct prerequisites remain satisfied; no Start, Delivery/end, Executable proof, Failure if, or dependency field changed. The recorded Complete tickets are RC-001/003/004/005/006/008/010/013/016/068. The incomplete in-progress tickets are RC-012/014/017/018/020; RC-002 remains the single blocked/unknown ticket. RC-018's Start and direct dependencies are satisfied. RC-011's direct dependency is complete, but its Start still requires container routing, which is unavailable. This graph reparse does not re-audit or re-accept previously completed task contracts.
- **Pre-fix reproduction:** Against `HEAD` `216bc65ae15c7990845ce157990ae3687e1d02d2`, the real-browser regression delayed `GET /api/version` by 6.5 seconds, let the real Elysia/SQLite `POST /api/actions` commit, then held its response. At the 11.5-second assertion from the user's click, the old client had already shown `Confirmed receipt`, because its ten-second timer began after capability negotiation and ID persistence. This demonstrates the missing click-to-result bound; it is not a regression against the previously recorded ten-second post-persistence window. The baseline run used the existing default `/tmp/remotecode.sqlite`; read-only reconciliation found exactly one matching test action (`c7a5d64a-813e-4f45-bf3a-ec23c8cc98b7`) and its request mapping (`3640c441-d250-433b-9710-2d4406f5c24b`), with `quick_check=ok`. The row was preserved, not deleted. Logs: `{SCRATCH}/rc018-click-deadline-red.log` and `{SCRATCH}/rc018-click-deadline-baseline-readback.log`.
- **Implementation:** The browser now starts one absolute 10,000 ms client deadline at action submission, before capability negotiation and request-ID persistence. Capability, POST, and each automatic receipt read use a shared-client request timeout no larger than the remaining budget; the POST has a 6,000 ms cap, receipt reads a 2,000 ms cap, and one delayed reread may wait up to 1,500 ms. If the deadline expires before the POST, the UI says no action was sent. If the POST may have committed, the stable ID stays pending, the result stays unknown, and a manual receipt read is required. No POST is replayed. Client abort does not imply the backend rolled back.
- **Review correction:** Reasoning reviewer `01a0ebda-0de0-7943-b878-b16f93bd3463` found a medium issue: a receipt read begun before expiry could complete after the deadline and clear the pending ID. It also found that an earlier editing pass had left two evidence headings with the same generated anchor. Added a post-read deadline check before parsing or applying a receipt, while preserving the 401 sign-out handling, and removed the superseded duplicate section. The new browser regression performs the actual committed receipt GET, then moves browser `Date.now()` beyond the absolute deadline before delivering the canonical response. Before the guard, the UI showed `Confirmed receipt`; after the guard, it remains unknown with the ID retained and manual lookup confirms the receipt. Red and green logs and isolated databases: `{SCRATCH}/rc018-late-receipt-red/` and `{SCRATCH}/rc018-late-receipt-green/`. The initial review was against the preceding patch. Final corrected-patch re-review `01a0ebeb-2279-7500-897c-779b3e8505e3` confirmed both findings closed and found no new concrete issue; the reviewer read the supplied logs but did not rerun tests or independently query SQLite.
- **Focused/full browser proof:** After the final request-aborting implementation, the focused Playwright test passed 1/1 in 10.7 seconds. It drives the real page and real backend: slow capability check, committed POST with a held response, click-relative timeout, pending-ID retention, zero automatic receipt lookups after expiry, and one manual canonical receipt read recovering the exact action. The full `apps/web/e2e/actions.spec.ts` passed 28 tests with 3 environment-dependent skips on Chromium, including the canonical-receipt-after-deadline regression (failed before the post-read guard, passed after) and the existing desktop/mobile recovery and reconnect journeys. Test outputs and isolated databases: `{SCRATCH}/rc018-click-deadline-final/` and `{SCRATCH}/rc018-actions-full-final-v2/`. The full-suite SQLite readback found one click-deadline and one late-receipt action, each with one request mapping, and `quick_check=ok`; ports 37117/37118 were released.

# RC-018 browser targeted revocation second automatic receipt read — partial — 2026-09-28

- **Repository/baseline:** Repository root `/Users/samuelfajreldines/dev/new-remote-code`; pre-change `HEAD` and origin branch were both `d2ae639939c8f69a28b09bcb7b3487c128364b19`. The only pre-existing untracked path was `scripts/__pycache__/`, which remains excluded. Baseline record: `{SCRATCH}/baseline-revoke.log`.
- **Eligibility/scope:** RC-018's Start and direct prerequisites RC-006, RC-008, RC-013, RC-004, and RC-016 remain satisfied. This is limited to browser `revoke_login` observation on the existing route. Task acceptance criteria are unchanged.
- **Behavior:** After the stable revocation request ID is persisted, the web app uses a six-second auth mutation client and a 13-second total post-persistence window. If a keyed targeted-revocation response is uncertain, it makes up to two credential-authorized, read-only receipt lookups using the passphrase captured only in the current request closure. A first 404 or typed `request_outcome_unknown` allows one reread after 1.5 seconds; the read client timeout is 3.5 seconds and the UI wait is capped by the remaining deadline. Only the exact six-field receipt for the request ID, target login ID, `revoke_login` kind, `login_revoked` outcome, valid canonical timestamps, and `expiresAt: null` clears the pending revocation. Timestamp strings must round-trip through `Date#toISOString()`; valid `Date` objects revived by Eden are also accepted. Missing or extra fields, missing timestamps, parseable non-ISO timestamps, stalled lookups, and mismatched values leave the operation unknown and sign-in disabled. No revocation POST is replayed and the password is not persisted. Logout remains manual.
- **Baseline reproduction:** At pre-change `HEAD`, the new desktop/mobile E2E committed a targeted revocation through the real Elysia/SQLite API and aborted the response. Both cases stayed on the initial unknown message and never issued an automatic receipt read; output: `{SCRATCH}/revoke-before.log`.
- **Browser proof:** Focused revocation coverage passed 14/14 on desktop and mobile: committed response loss with a delayed canonical receipt; pre-acceptance abort and two not-found reads; two stalled reads after commit; a canonical receipt with the wrong target; matching receipts with missing `createdAt` and parseable non-ISO `createdAt`; and explicit recovery after wrong-password denial. Each mutation path asserts one revocation POST. Only the exact, complete canonical `login_revoked` receipt clears pending state; missing, stalled, malformed, and mismatched results keep the pending ID and block sign-in. The committed case reads the receipt back as `login_revoked` with `sessionStatus: "not_applicable"` and confirms the browser session is 401. Current focused output: `{SCRATCH}/revoke-focused-final-current.log`; the earlier missing-field-only run is retained separately.
- **Full browser suite:** The current full 68-case browser suite passed 65 tests with 3 environment-dependent skips using the one-worker shared API/SQLite fixture. Output: `{SCRATCH}/revoke-browser-full-current.log`. Earlier screenshots of confirmed and unknown browser states are `{SCRATCH}/browser.png`, `{SCRATCH}/revoke-desktop.png`, `{SCRATCH}/revoke-mobile.png`, `{SCRATCH}/revoke-unknown-desktop.png`, and `{SCRATCH}/revoke-unknown-mobile.png`; no layout or styling changed in this correction.
- **Other checks:** `bun run test` passed 97 tests/650 assertions; `bun run typecheck` passed. Current outputs: `{SCRATCH}/revoke-unit-final-current.log` and `{SCRATCH}/revoke-typecheck-final-current.log`. The six-field/missing-field regression previously failed in both viewports when receipt-shape validation was removed. For this correction, the source was temporarily changed to accept any finite `new Date(value)` result instead of requiring `timestamp.toISOString() === value`; both desktop and mobile non-ISO tests then failed because the UI falsely cleared pending state and the receipt button disappeared. The strict source was restored with SHA-256 `7e2c99d8d3c311a20ab6f60e8f93209ed0379ff41141d69141673e8bc977b4b4` matching before and after. Evidence: `{SCRATCH}/receipt-non-iso-mutation.log` and `{SCRATCH}/receipt-non-iso-mutation-result.log`. `python3 scripts/check-doc-links.py` and `git diff --check` passed after removing the review finding; output: `{SCRATCH}/revoke-handoff-post-review-fix.log`.
- **Review correction:** Reviewer `01a0e9ae-3126-7581-a89b-7051c35db8a8` previously found that a receipt missing canonical timestamp fields could clear the pending ID; the exact-field and timestamp checks and missing-`createdAt` test addressed that finding. Reviewer `01a0e9c5-f734-7e91-8715-25c7c29bc012` then found that permissive date parsing also accepts parseable non-ISO strings such as `September 28, 2026`. `isAuthReceiptTimestamp` now requires exact ISO round-trip for strings while accepting valid Eden-revived `Date` values, and the new non-ISO E2E verifies the outcome stays unknown. Full-diff reviewer `01a0e9d8-e89a-7990-a03d-bcd290f3dcf9` found no code defect but identified a stray standalone backtick in the prior evidence line; it was removed. The reviewer read the supplied logs but did not rerun commands or independently verify the reported hash/provenance. Follow-up review confirmed the marker is closed and found that the phrase “final documentation/whitespace checks remain pending” contradicted the recorded pass result above; that phrase was removed. The final follow-up `01a0e9e3-ac9f-7260-bbdc-ccb191e11146` confirmed both handoff findings are closed with no remaining status contradiction. After resolving that wording inconsistency, parent reran `python3 scripts/check-doc-links.py`, `git diff --check`, and heading/status assertions; all passed. Output: `{SCRATCH}/revoke-handoff-status-reconciled.log`.
- **Publication and CI:** Commit `f33e2f82847fa35f6629a406953e046319faec67` (`fix(web): reject malformed auth recovery receipts`) was pushed to `origin/checkpoint/rc002-linux-runtime-evidence` and read back at the exact same SHA. Runs `36485610362` and `36485603705` both matched that SHA and passed `static` and `test`. Parent readback: `{SCRATCH}/checkpoint-f33e2f8-readback.log`. This is repository CI evidence, not RC-018 acceptance or deployed behavior.
- **X boundary:** No second account check or post was attempted for this incremental correctness slice; it is not a new completed user-facing milestone. The prior `mac-use` close returned `closed:false` with `hasTab:false` afterward, leaving physical removal unverified; no replacement tab was opened to avoid interfering with a possible user-owned tab. The prior check and retained draft are recorded below.
- **Limits/next:** RC-018 remains in progress; logout, other mutation consumers, Android, shared retry/jitter budgets, and total click/gateway deadlines remain open. No deployed behavior is claimed. RC-002 remains unknown and untouched. Next eligible assessment is RC-020 cost above 1 GiB through the shipped gate path, on macOS only; this cannot substitute for the production Linux proof.

# RC-018 browser login second automatic receipt read — partial — 2026-09-28

- **Repository/baseline:** Repository root `/Users/samuelfajreldines/dev/new-remote-code`; pre-change `HEAD` and `origin/checkpoint/rc002-linux-runtime-evidence` were both `6ad8fe252bf6b09c4f698c5dd57ac19fd5f985bb`. At task entry, `apps/web/e2e/auth-recovery.spec.ts` and `apps/web/src/features/actions/App.tsx` already contained uncommitted RC-018 edits; those were preserved and completed. The pre-existing untracked `scripts/__pycache__/` is excluded from the checkpoint.
- **Eligibility and scope:** RC-018's Start is met by the recorded persistent backend and reviewed failure contract; direct prerequisites RC-006, RC-008, RC-013, RC-004, and RC-016 are complete. This is a partial browser-login continuation only. Original delivery, failure, and executable-proof criteria are unchanged.
- **Behavior:** After an uncertain keyed login, the browser first checks whether its actual session cookie matches the request ID. If not, it makes at most two read-only receipt lookups inside the 13-second post-persistence deadline. The second lookup follows the first 404 or typed `request_outcome_unknown` result after 1.5 seconds; each lookup has the existing 3.5-second client timeout and is clamped by the absolute deadline. A matching receipt does not authenticate the UI without its cookie. Neither a missing receipt nor a stalled lookup resends the login; the pending ID remains and sign-in stays disabled.
- **Failure-path correction:** The initial browser fixture returned an empty 200 body but preserved `Set-Cookie`, so Chromium signed in and bypassed receipt recovery. The corrected fixture executes the real API login, clears the browser cookie, and aborts the response after commit. The first lookup returns 404 and the second reaches the real API. Another test makes both receipt reads stall; final UI state remains unknown rather than merely asserting the initial pending message.
- **Browser proof:** Bun 1.3.13, Node 22.23.2, Playwright 1.63.0, Chromium, macOS arm64. Focused Chromium coverage passed 8/8 at the spec's desktop 1280×900 and mobile 390×844 viewports. The delayed-receipt path asserted one login POST, two receipt lookups, canonical `session_created` receipt readback, browser session GET 401, no connected/private UI, and retained pending ID. Missing receipt and two stalled reads both kept the result unknown and did not enable another login. The stalled case completed in about 9 seconds. Traces and screenshots: `{SCRATCH}/auth-focused-final2.log`, `{SCRATCH}/playwright-auth-final2/`, `{SCRATCH}/browser-desktop.png`, and `{SCRATCH}/browser-mobile.png`.
- **Full browser suite:** The first two-worker run logged 50 passed, 3 skipped, and 3 failed, including two action-recovery waits and a receipt whose session status changed during concurrent tests. The same 56-case suite passed serially with 53 passed and 3 environment-dependent skips. The E2E runner shares one API process, SQLite database, and local auth identity; `playwright.config.ts` now fixes it at one worker. The standard `bun run test:e2e -- --output={SCRATCH}/playwright-full-configured` run then passed 53 with the same 3 environment-dependent skips. Outputs: `{SCRATCH}/browser-full.log`, `{SCRATCH}/browser-full-serial.log`, `{SCRATCH}/browser-full-configured.log`.
- **Other local checks:** `bun run test` passed 97 tests/650 assertions; `bun run typecheck` passed. `python3 scripts/check-doc-links.py` checked README and all 3 plan pages; `git diff --check` passed after the handoff edits. Outputs: `{SCRATCH}/unit-final.log`, `{SCRATCH}/typecheck-final.log`, `{SCRATCH}/handoff-links-review.log`, `{SCRATCH}/handoff-whitespace-review.log`, and `{SCRATCH}/handoff-review-checks.log`. No deployed behavior is claimed.
- **Independent review:** Reviewer `01a0e963-c8e3-7cd3-8a89-c6e278da9859` inspected the saved diff, relevant source, task contract, and supplied logs; it found no material defect. The reviewer did not rerun checks or independently verify command provenance; the parent captured the local outputs above. Whole-task acceptance remains open.
- **Publication/CI:** Commit `21d0a2eaecd73f286b95879dedd9a931d75c7ea6` (`feat(web): retry uncertain login receipt lookups`) was pushed to `origin/checkpoint/rc002-linux-runtime-evidence`; `git ls-remote` read back the same SHA. GitHub runs `36469962620` and `36469955678` both matched that SHA and passed their `static` and `test` jobs.
- **X check:** Signed-in profile confirmed `@samfajreldines`; the latest own post was displayed as `12:47 pm · 27 Sep 2026`, so more than one hour had elapsed. Draft (216 characters): `I changed web sign-in to check for a receipt again after an uncertain login instead of resending. It stays signed out until the cookie matches. Desktop and mobile browser tests pass. Not deployed. #buildingRemoteCode`. The opened route showed the existing post's `Post your reply` composer and a `Reply` button, not a new-post composer. No text was entered and no post was submitted. `browser_close` returned `closed:false`, `released:true`; the subsequent `browser_status` reported `hasTab:false`. Physical tab removal is unverified; no replacement tab or retry was opened. Captured account, latest-post and cleanup observations: `{SCRATCH}/x-check.log`.

# RC-018 bounded second automatic action receipt read — partial — 2026-09-28

- **Scope:** One bounded RC-018 slice: the web action screen now makes up to two automatic receipt reads after an uncertain POST (immediate + one delayed reread after an increasing wait), still inside the existing ten-second post-persistence window, with no POST replay. RC-002 was not called or investigated.
- **Correction/review:** Independent review `01a0e904` PASS on test validity and regressions but FAIL on deadline correctness: each read was capped at 2s without clamping to the remaining deadline, so a slow POST plus two stalled reads and the wait could run ~11.5s past the 10s deadline. Fixed by clamping each read's cap to `min(2_000, remaining)`; logout/reload still invalidate via `isCurrent`, and 401 on either attempt still hides the session.
- **Proof:** New browser test makes the first automatic GET return 404 and the second reach the backend: asserts 1 POST, 2 GETs, cleared pending storage, one durable action, and visible confirmed receipt — passed (2.4s). Existing stalled-lookup test (both reads stalled, unknown outcome, no resend) passed (6.4s); existing immediate-confirm test (single lookup, `lookups==1`) passed, proving no extra read on the fast path. Full API/client suite 97/650 green; typecheck green. Evidence: `{SCRATCH}/rc018-delayed-e2e.log`, `{SCRATCH}/rc018-multiread-unit.log`.
- **Boundary:** This is two reads with increasing wait, not full jitter/backoff or gateway/click deadlines. Android, other mutation consumers, total click/gateway deadlines, and full RC-018 acceptance remain open. No phase or dependent ticket is accepted.

- **Follow-up review correction (same date):** Independent reviewer `01a0e881-19cf-7e00-ac9b-77199855e190` found the unconditional `POST /api/auth/receipts/:requestId/lookup` skip unsafe: with a session cookie, `sessionUserId` → `readSession` deletes an expired session row (`apps/api/src/features/auth.ts:53-64,207-216`), which is a SQLite write that bypassed the probe. The gate now skips only password-only lookups (`!sessionToken(request)`); cookie-carrying lookups probe. A standalone check created a session, expired its row, corrupted a noncritical page, then sent a cookie lookup: it returned 503 `storage_unavailable` with the session row count unchanged (1→1). The committed probe-scope test now also asserts a cookie-carrying lookup returns 404 and adds one probe call. Final: storage suite 7/58, full suite 97/645, typecheck, doc links, `git diff --check` green. Evidence: `{SCRATCH}/rc020-storage-final.log`, `{SCRATCH}/rc020-full-final.log`.
- **Root/baseline:** `/Users/samuelfajreldines/dev/new-remote-code`; pre-fix `HEAD` `19770b0dfe845e6c37e994d457258f35f3c2e034`. The existing disk-full/lock gate and RC-020 task contract were preserved. RC-002 was not called or investigated.
- **Differential reproduction:** Added a real Elysia/SQLite case that signs in, writes one baseline action, corrupts a noncritical SQLite page after API creation, sends one distinct keyed `POST /api/actions` before any readiness request, then reads the action/mapping state. Before the fix, the test failed because actions increased from 1 to 2. The source path confirmed the cause: `app.ts` returned early while `storageUnavailable` was false, skipping the integrity worker.
- **Correction/green:** A global `onBeforeHandle` readiness gate is registered before feature plugins so it is inherited by their routes and runs after route matching and validation. It checks reachable mutations that may proceed: login/revocation and requests carrying a syntactically valid session cookie. It skips anonymous private calls without such a cookie, nonexistent paths, and `POST /api/auth/receipts/:requestId/lookup`, which is read-only. The existing bounded Worker runs `PRAGMA quick_check`; a failed observation leaves the storage latch degraded and returns 503 before the mutation handler. The corruption regression proves the prior action and mapping remain, the action count stays 1, the attempted request has no mapping, and corrupted database bytes do not change.
- **Review finding and correction:** The prior `onRequest` gate launched the Worker on every POST before authentication or route matching. The new regression failed against it: anonymous `401`, unmatched `404`, and receipt lookup `404` produced `readinessCalls=3` instead of 0. Registering the replacement hook after feature plugins initially missed login (`200` but 0 probes); placing it before the plugins made the global hook reach their routes. The final test also gives the unmatched path a syntactically valid dummy cookie, then verifies it still causes no probe. It verifies login calls the Worker once and a real authenticated action calls it once more. A valid-format cookie is not proof of an active session; session-backed writes still undergo normal route authorization.
- **Focused/full regression checks:** The new probe-scope test passed 1 test/6 expectations; the complete storage suite passed 7 tests/56 expectations; `bun run test` passed 97 tests/643 expectations on Bun 1.3.13; `bun run typecheck`, `python3 scripts/check-doc-links.py`, and `git diff --check` passed. These checks validate the revised gate; they do not replace the separate Linux/tmpfs or installed-iOS evidence below.
- **Performance/concurrency measurement:** Bun 1.3.13 on macOS arm64, real Elysia `/api/actions` requests with the production middleware, and SQLite files of 1,245,184 bytes, 18,243,584 bytes, and 72,617,984 bytes (nominal 1, 16, and 64 MiB payloads). Five sequential write maxima were 8.62 ms, 13.49 ms, and 34.04 ms; eight simultaneous writes completed in 11.44 ms, 17.31 ms, and 39.72 ms. Each case returned 201 for all 13 distinct writes, with 13 owner-scoped receipts and `PRAGMA quick_check=ok`. This does not establish performance above 64 MiB or on production storage. Output: `{SCRATCH}/rc020-quick-check-size-benchmark.log`.
- **Regression checks after correction:** `bun run test` passed 96 tests/637 expectations with all test databases under the private scratch `TMPDIR`; `bun run typecheck` passed. The Linux/amd64 48 MiB tmpfs proof was repeated against the corrected API: actual ENOSPC, readiness/write 503, the earlier receipt and mapping unchanged after restart, the attempted ID absent, and a distinct post-recovery write accepted. The owned container was removed. A Release iOS XCUITest after the correction passed the real SQLite lock → “Host not ready” → “Host ready” → native action/receipt → logout journey; readback found one keyed native action/mapping and zero sessions. Captures: `{SCRATCH}/rc020-pre-readiness-green.log`, `{SCRATCH}/rc020-full-suite-postfix.log`, `{SCRATCH}/rc020-linux-full-volume-postfix.log`, `{SCRATCH}/rc020-native-health-postfix.log`, and `{SCRATCH}/native-health-postfix/xcodebuild-test.log`.
- **Acceptance boundary:** RC-020 remains In Progress. The correction closes the specifically reproduced first-write-before-readiness path; full volume/lock/restart and production Linux-host or larger database behavior remain separate evidence. No phase or dependent ticket is accepted by this patch.

# RC-018 native automatic login attempt retracted — 2026-09-27

- **Scope/decision:** The reviewed/pushed web login and native manual-login recovery remain unchanged. A proposed automatic native 13-second post-persistence session/receipt observation was developed but **never committed**. Reasoning review `01a0e5cf-edb6-75d3-b0a3-8c99b53671e6` identified a durable-state race: asynchronous removal of the pending login ID can finish after the deadline while the UI stays signed out; a late definitive rejection can erase it too. Attempted deadline checks did not make the AsyncStorage removal and UI state atomic, so the change was retracted rather than claimed safe.
- **Proof and next:** The restored installed iPhone 16 Pro baseline passed all five auth/action/recovery tests with SQLite readback; original simulator remained Shutdown and the disposable test simulator was deleted. This verifies no shipped regression, **not automatic native login observation**. The next implementation must preserve the durable ID through expiry/storage failure or complete an authoritative matching-cookie transition without late implicit authentication; reproduce a delayed storage completion and lost-cookie case before claiming the feature. Native manual receipt lookup and explicit orphan revocation are still available. Other RC-018 and RC-020 requirements stay open.

# RC-018 browser login observation and RC-020 readiness latch — partial — 2026-09-27

- **Web login:** A keyed login ID remains in tab storage before the POST. The browser caps the POST at six seconds, then first validates the current session's `loginRequestId`; if no matching cookie is confirmed, one read-only receipt lookup uses the transient in-function password. A historical `session_created` receipt without its matching cookie never authenticates the UI, repeats POST, or silently revokes the old login. The automatic path has an absolute 13-second post-persistence deadline across POST/session/receipt waits; requests arriving after that deadline or an auth-epoch change cannot clear the pending login or restore private state. Logout/revocation and other mutation observation budgets remain open. Independent reviewer `01a0e5ae-4557-7062-a247-1d5e9ac21359` identified the unclamped late session/receipt observation; after fixing both waits and late-state application, follow-ups `01a0e5b0-7467-7901-9ee5-9b02137b082d` and `01a0e5b1-b756-7741-bfc3-ee7f2301ec17` found no residual focused defect. They did not execute browser tests. The final frozen integrated Chromium suite passed 50 cases with three environment skips against a new SQLite database; earlier whole-suite attempts overlapped backend edits and are not used as acceptance evidence.
- **Storage mitigation and diagnosed regression:** Post-start corruption of an already-running SQLite database previously produced readiness 503 but still allowed a keyed action 201 and receipt 200. A failed readiness check now marks mutations unavailable. A single shared, bounded readiness probe is reused by health requests and mutations **only while blocked**; a healthy recheck clears the block, and healthy-state writes incur no preflight worker. A first integration run caught a real liveness regression: the native test-only HTTP POST used to release an exclusive lock was itself refused after readiness failed. The fixture now releases its externally held test lock through a cookie-authorized test-only GET outside the production `/api` API; no production mutation bypass was added. A second test caught a healthy DB remaining blocked without a further health poll; mutation-triggered recheck resolved it. Scoped real Elysia/SQLite/Eden tests passed 53 cases/421 assertions, including runtime corruption after a failed probe, transient lock release, one committed duplicate action, auth receipts, and previous storage mutations. Reviewer `01a0e5b8-cf7e-7782-b36f-63c6f31511ce` found no further concrete defect in the bounded probe on source inspection; `01a0e5b4-a9d1-7390-8501-283bcf456ef1` found no defect in the test-only lock control.
- **Still open:** Corruption introduced after startup and followed immediately by a write *before any readiness observation* can still return 201 and a receipt; in-flight tampering races also have no acceptance proof. RC-020 remains unaccepted. Both 13-second login and ten-second action windows start after request-ID persistence, not at the user's click. Login/logout/storage consumers and a production gateway still lack total deadlines and multi-attempt delay/jitter; RC-018 also remains unaccepted. The installed-iOS readiness regression initially failed on the original simulator because a pending action from an earlier failed test remained in its device storage; its Submit button was disabled. A fresh task-owned iPhone 16 Pro simulator then passed the real SQLite lock → 503/`Host not ready` → release → 200/`Host ready` → native action/receipt journey. SQLite readback confirmed one action with a stable request mapping and zero remaining sessions. The disposable simulator was deleted and the original remained Shutdown. This local proof does not establish immediate corruption-before-probe safety.

# RC-018 bounded web action observation — partial — 2026-09-27

- **Baseline/scope:** After backend history checkpoint `171e1352686f3258077ebd5038550e81ed5267d0`, the existing browser action flow now automatically consults one read-only receipt after an uncertain POST rather than waiting for a manual button click. Its pending ID was already persisted before POST. The POST client cap is six seconds, and the subsequent read has at most the remaining time in a ten-second window starting after persistence; no POST is replayed. If the receipt remains missing/offline/stalled, the spinner ends and the pending identity remains for explicit later lookup. Preflight capability and storage checks are outside this limited window.
- **Red diagnoses and proof:** Initial browser runs reused orphaned task-owned API/Vite servers on test ports and an uncontrolled database, causing unrelated sign-in outcomes to remain unknown. Those exact orphan processes were stopped without touching unrelated resources. A fresh scratch-scoped SQLite host then passed the new automatic lookup journey. The full browser suite first passed 39 cases with one failure: an older manual-401 test expected a pending button after the new automatic read had already confirmed the receipt. That test now makes the automatic read return 404 before invoking its manual authorization transition. Independent reviewer `01a0e57f-b011-70a1-9535-50569b185bb3` found a real automatic-401 privacy defect: a read rejection kept private UI visible. A new real-browser test reproduced it; the code now invalidates auth state and hides the session, preserving the pending ID. Follow-up reviewer `01a0e583-4b31-7243-a646-d1892611b67d` found the focused defect closed. The fresh complete Chromium suite then passed 41 cases with three environment skips; both short-session expiry cases passed separately. A separately stalled automatic receipt read stopped waiting within the bounded period with one POST and one persisted action. Desktop/mobile affected routes and existing auth/action flows were exercised; no hosted gateway or installed-native automatic checking was proved.
- **Open gates:** The ten-second window begins after request-ID persistence, not the user's click; only one automatic read is attempted, so multi-attempt increasing delay/jitter is not implemented. Hosted gateway/server total deadlines, native automatic lookup, storage mutation UI consumers and full RC-018 acceptance remain open. RC-020 post-start corruption can still falsely accept a write and is not made safe by these client observations.

# RC-020 storage degradation backend and web health — partial — 2026-09-27

- **Baseline:** `7d826330bd1bf6079f68352f9674231e84deb394` is the reviewed/pushed preceding checkpoint. This storage slice changes the startup corruption guard, SQLite readiness probe, shared Eden health response handling, and isolated corruption/lock integration tests. It does not alter the Distill request whose outcome remains unknown.
- **Observed before correction:** A deliberately corrupted SQLite copy passed the old readiness check and an authenticated write returned 201. The first mutation-level preflight caused an existing concurrent action test to return `[200,200,201,500]` instead of `[200,200,200,201]`; it was removed. The real health client threw on an intentional 503 `{status:"not_ready"}`, and a desktop browser waited for a degraded label that never appeared. These failures informed the bounded corrections rather than being labeled environmental success.
- **Current local proof:** Startup integrity inspection rejects mutations against the damaged test copy with 503; the test compares the *affected copy's* main-file bytes before and after and confirms the untouched source's previous value. A separate connection's exclusive lock makes readiness return 503, blocks the write, and after release the same API returns readiness 200 and accepts a valid write. The shared Eden client translates only an actual 503 not-ready health response; other errors still throw. Focused Elysia/Eden/SQLite auth/action/storage regression suite: 32 passed, 260 assertions; configured link check, typecheck and whitespace passed. Local Chromium drove real Vite/API sign-in, health ready → locked not ready → ready, and sign-out without page errors at 1280×900 and 390×844. Test-only listeners and browser processes exited. The independent reviewer found and closed a mutation-preflight concurrency issue and a corrupt-copy evidence gap; a conditional permanent-lock finding was retracted after the same-instance recovery test.
- **Full-volume follow-up on macOS:** An owned, fixed 128 MiB HFS+ image held the real API SQLite database and keyed-action receipt. Writing actual 4 KiB blocks reached `ENOSPC` at 130,977,792 filler bytes. Before the readiness correction, `/api/health/ready` wrongly returned 200 while the single new keyed action returned 500 `unable to open database file`. The worker now rejects zero available filesystem blocks. The checked-in `scripts/run-storage-full-proof.sh` reproduced the green result: readiness 503 `not_ready`, the single attempted write 500 with no receipt/action after filler removal and API restart, the previous action and its mapping unchanged, `PRAGMA quick_check` okay, and a new distinct write 201. The owned device was detached and its mount/run directory removed; the test never replayed the uncertain request. This is local macOS full-volume evidence, not Linux or installed-native proof. The 500 response is not a confirmed rollback on its own; durable readback resolves this synthetic attempt.
- **Installed iOS follow-up:** The iPhone 16 Pro simulator ran a test-only wrapper around the real Elysia/SQLite routes. An authenticated observer held an actual exclusive SQLite lock: readiness returned 503 and the installed app displayed `Host not ready`. After release readiness returned 200 and the app displayed `Host ready`, signed in, submitted its own action, showed the confirmed receipt and signed out. The runner read back exactly one `native-health-*` action, one matching `action_requests` mapping, a login/logout pair and zero sessions. The first attempt to prove native submission revealed a test interaction failure: tapping static text had left the keyboard open and XCUITest injected a trailing character; an onPress probe showed Submit was never invoked and SQLite had no row. The final test taps the keyboard's Return key, waits for dismissal, checks exact input, and the installed app submits successfully. Action autocorrection is disabled to preserve intended text. The five existing installed auth/action journeys also passed again, including native submission and restart recovery. Reviewer `01a0e531-bffa-7012-a672-d1f8b8425255` identified the initial proof gap; follow-up `01a0e553-9a33-7090-806b-9c7567ab7a4e` found it closed for the corrected test path. The reviewers did not rerun the installed tests.
- **Runtime-corruption counterexample:** With an already-running API and healthy database, a noncritical SQLite page was overwritten after startup in an isolated test copy. `/api/health/ready` returned 503, but an authenticated keyed `POST /api/actions` returned 201 and its durable receipt lookup returned 200. This falsifies a broad “no writes to a corrupted database” claim. The reproduction is local and not yet a committed regression test. A synchronous full integrity scan on every write was not added because its cost grows with database size and no bounded, concurrency-safe approach has been proven. Preserve this as an open RC-020 defect, not a passing result; continue independent planned work while designing a verified fail-closed write boundary.
- **Open gates:** The startup guard does not stop mutations after new corruption *after* startup; prior-value recovery from the corrupted copy, full durable write receipts under every degradation, Linux full-volume proof and post-deploy behavior are not verified. RC-020 therefore remains in progress, not accepted. Some existing test fixtures use hard-coded system temporary paths, so the unrestricted full suite was not rerun under the goal's scratch restriction; the scratch-safe focused subset and existing auth/client evidence have distinct versions. Commit/push/CI and committed-only build for this storage delta remain separate until checked.

- **Delivery:** Auth-local SQLite receipts atomically accompany keyed login/logout and targeted login revocation. Login replay returns canonical history without new cookie/session; expiry/revocation never remints it. A revocation can fence an unseen login ID, preventing delayed acceptance. An old accepted logout cannot delete newer sessions or clear their cookie on replay. Lookup requires actual authority and separates historical outcome from current validity. Socket identities captured at authenticated open allow post-commit all-user or targeted revocation without querying deleted rows. Validation errors omit credentials; password-bearing proxy routes reject normalized/trailing-slash plaintext ingress.
- **Proof:** The initial repeated-ID test issued four cookies instead of one (`red.log`). Fresh Eden clients recover dropped HTTP bodies after API-instance restart; `auth-process.test.ts` separately launches the shipped `apps/api/src/index.ts` in three real processes and confirms durable login/revocation outcomes and no resurrected session. Typecheck, links and whitespace passed.

No production shutdown fix is claimed. See `ingress-red.log`, `web-regressions.log` and `socket-shutdown-diagnosis.log`.

Follow-up review identified an additional URN alias accepted by the installed UUID validator. It was reproduced (`urn-red.log`), then the shared auth schema was restricted to the 36-character representation. Login, lookup, revocation and logout now reject URNs with 422 while retaining only the original session/receipt. The four affected browser cases passed after normalization, including both short-session expiry cases (`final-web.log`); 23 distinct browser cases are verified overall.
