## RC-008 — durable storage foundation (accepted) — 2026-09-25

- **Status:** Accepted against the original delivery, executable-proof, and failure criteria after exact-diff independent review. The database, workspace records, history, and persistent profile records live on a named volume and restore on process start; no confirmed data loss occurred on a different image ID, and locked-database writes were not confirmed or persisted.
- **Implementation:** `apps/api/src/features/storage.ts` initializes SQLite tables for workspaces, profiles, and history on the configured database path and exposes authenticated list/create, profile read/update, and workspace history read/write routes. History inserts require ownership of the parent workspace. Readiness now checks the action, workspace, history, and profile tables. `prototype/compose.yaml` uses `/var/lib/remotecode/remotecode.sqlite` on the named `remotecode-data` volume. Local development defaults to `/tmp/remotecode.sqlite`.
- **Local checks on final source:** `bun run typecheck` passed; `bun test apps/api/src/app.test.ts` passed 16 tests/80 expectations, including domain record restoration after API recreation, missing-domain-table readiness, exclusive-lock write rejection, and recovery. Full `bun run test:e2e` passed 2 tests; the Linux-guest-only case was skipped without its separate RC-003 guest configuration. `bunx vite build --config apps/web/vite.config.ts`, `python3 scripts/check-doc-links.py`, and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Named-volume recovery proof:** Project `rc008-domain-proof-1790364667`, container ID `fef6c0b0abc33b92bc29d8c82bd746276fa8df1a25bd508655ad477dbf30ad9f`, named volume `rc008-domain-proof-1790364667_remotecode-data`, base image `sha256:13e27c5ca5b48cb8079200f1262fa48cb2160880aedb68d1372c3d74ff8641db`. On Linux amd64 emulation under a macOS arm64 host, workspace ID `6759d565-971c-481a-adb5-0d53e69a2f77` / name `named-volume workspace`, history ID `3d9cc6ae-c86b-4355-ac1a-e81e91453cb8` / content `named-volume history content`, and profile user `local` / display name `named-volume profile` were written, then the container was removed and recreated with the same volume. Readback returned the exact IDs and contents.
- **Failure proof:** With a real exclusive SQLite lock held, readiness returned 503 and liveness 200. An authenticated workspace write returned 401 because the session lookup could not read the locked database; after unlock, readiness returned 200 and the workspace list was unchanged. Thus no write was confirmed or added while persistence was unavailable. This test does not imply storage-degradation UI behavior, which remains in later tasks.
- **Image replacement proof:** Before replacement, action receipt `edb4287b-f098-4e57-a1b7-ce9a7757800e` was committed. A distinct image ID `sha256:7f3bb43cfafd88009ae2e81e9aa8bde5df577b23d344287f5980aa214ee01b68` then restored the exact workspace, history, profile, and action receipt using the same named volume. The replacement was derived from the base image by adding a label; application filesystem layers were unchanged. This establishes volume state independence from container/image identity but does not prove an application-version upgrade. Both images ran as Linux amd64 under emulation, not on a native amd64 host.
- **Browser behavior check:** Started the local API and web app with the test-only passphrase from `playwright.config.ts`, opened `http://127.0.0.1:37118` in Chrome, signed in, submitted `manual browser checkpoint verification`, and observed confirmed receipt `e3fc05fe-910e-42eb-9a4c-504dd62e743f` and the same record in action history. Signed out and observed the sign-in screen. The E2E test also sets the viewport to 390×844 and checks no horizontal overflow; desktop run uses 1280×900. No manual browser resize control was available.
- **Independent review:** The first pass was not relied upon because it did not have the exact diff/current artifacts. The follow-up reasoning-model review inspected the supplied 1,218-line source patch, acceptance wording, and both fresh proof logs. It found no concrete defect on changed lines and no demonstrated unmet RC-008 criterion. It explicitly cautioned that the replacement image changed identity/label, not application layers, so this is not an application-version migration proof. It also treated the separate Chromium user-data directory as outside RC-008's stated profile-record scope; secret exposure in the agent environment remains a separate RC-015 concern, not an RC-008 finding.
- **Historical next work (superseded by the accepted RC-010 checkpoint below):** At that point RC-010 was to continue against the persistent API substrate and remained unaccepted pending session/auth, Linux, and independent security evidence. RC-009 remains gated on RC-002 and full RC-008. RC-002's timed-out external effect remains unknown and was not retried.

## RC-010 — host authentication and private routes (accepted) — 2026-09-25

- **Acceptance contract and decision:** RC-010 is accepted against its original, unchanged delivery/proof/failure criteria: backend-validated sessions, logout and expiry; anonymous/private-route rejection; and expired sessions never accessing private data. The implementation and behavior evidence are recorded below. RC-008 is accepted and its named-volume API satisfies this task's start condition. No acceptance criterion was reduced.
- **Independent review:** Reasoning reviewer `01a0db17-6f9c-7962-a10c-061f20ca10bd` reviewed complete patch `44bf07a1b863f51a141c46900c766d0e922836fffbd5596de1a0c44d7ec79ad3`, found no concrete defect, and confirmed that task/evidence/handoff status consistently remained in progress until this clean review. Earlier implementation-only review `01a0db0e-82b3-77a0-ad0f-527ef534028c` did not cover the complete patch. The reviewer did not independently run tests or reproduce Linux results; those acceptance proofs are the recorded execution evidence below. This acceptance-record update will receive a final exact-patch review before commit/push.
- **Latest-image proof:** On Linux amd64 image `sha256:398dfc2639647b24469cf639efc73bc55db69cd139233bf574b6427103aeca54` under macOS arm64 emulation, guest Chromium via Node Playwright completed valid login, authenticated WebSocket connection, and private read (200); action receipt `8a4bbf91-d3fe-48d4-afa7-ae139581db35` was created/read; UI logout returned to sign-in and private read returned 401. On the same image and retained named volume with 2500 ms TTL, receipt `8c8a0fb3-ad8a-4bbc-b899-6f43aa8444a2` was created; private access was 200 before expiry, then session and private routes were 401 and the UI receipt was cleared. Anonymous private access was 401. Separate-peer canonical and dot-segment cleartext logins returned 403. The earlier CDP timeout was specific to invoking Playwright through Bun, whose Origin Chrome rejected; Node connected to the unchanged image. No application startup change was made.
- **Post-correction local checks:** Typecheck passed; API tests passed 20 tests/99 expectations; full E2E passed 7 with 3 configuration-dependent skips; focused expiry and WebSocket-loss E2E passed 2; Vite build, local documentation links and whitespace checks passed.
- **Environment limits:** Linux checks were amd64 emulation on macOS arm64, not native Linux. No external TLS-terminating proxy was available; HTTPS Secure-cookie behavior is unit-tested directly. No restart-recovery claim is made; the in-place Xvfb restart failed, so proof used fresh containers with the same image and named volume. The volume is retained, and proof containers are stopped.
- **Independent review findings and corrections:** Earlier review findings led to removing `X-Forwarded-Proto` trust, exact WebSocket `Origin` validation, and readiness checks for `sessions`; ignore protections and the RC-008 accepted-evidence link were restored. Follow-up review found that URL hostnames alone let an HTTP client forge `Host: localhost`, and that logout did not promptly revoke other open tabs. Login now permits cleartext only when Bun reports the actual peer address as `127.0.0.1` or `::1`; missing peer identity fails closed. Logout revokes sockets for the token with close code 4401, and the web client clears authenticated state, receipt, and private history on that close. A later review questioned host-browser login, idle-session expiry, and an in-flight write response after logout. The host-browser finding was disproved for the supported UI route: the actual host-side Playwright journey against the Linux image's published web port passed through Vite's same-origin `/api` proxy; direct HTTP access to the separately published API port correctly returned 403. For the valid findings, authenticated WebSockets now schedule closure at persisted session expiry and the UI clears private state on that close. An auth epoch guards action, list, and socket responses so a pending request from an earlier session cannot repopulate private state after logout or a subsequent login. `README.md` documents that remote TLS-terminating-proxy access remains unsupported until authenticated ingress identity exists.
- **Focused local results:** `bun run typecheck` passed. API suite passed 19 tests / 97 expectations, including forged-Host rejection, persisted sessions, logout, expiry, unavailable durable storage, Secure cookie for HTTPS, exact WebSocket origin, and automatic closure of an idle WebSocket at session expiry. Standard browser E2E passed 4 tests; the short-TTL expiry and RC-003 guest/external exchange were skipped in that run because their distinct configurations were not set. With `REMOTECODE_AUTH_SESSION_TTL_MS=2500`, the expiry UI E2E passed separately; the delayed-write-after-logout UI E2E passed in the standard run. A host-side Playwright run against the final Linux image's published web port passed the sign-in/action/mobile journey; directly calling its separately published API port returned 403, while calls through Vite's same-origin API proxy succeeded. Vite production build, documentation links, whitespace, shell/Node syntax, ignore rules, and Compose config with a disposable test-only passphrase passed. The earlier generic `bun run test` discovery issue remains fixed by targeting `apps/api/src`.
- **Final Linux API runtime proof:** Rebuilt the corrected source as Linux amd64 image `remotecode-rc010-final-checkpoint-20260925:local`, manifest digest `sha256:9730b0fd3d60db6eae4a3881d60f43284c57b6b4df7b31e7ea5d7825be9c9ca9`. Host: macOS arm64; container: Linux x86_64 under OrbStack emulation. On an isolated Docker network and temporary named volume, readiness returned 200; anonymous private route 401; a separate non-loopback container spoofing `Host: localhost` on HTTP was rejected with 403 and no `Set-Cookie`; actual loopback-peer HTTP login returned 200; valid private route 200; forced persisted-session expiry/private route 401; valid session after container recreation returned 200; logout returned 204; post-logout private route returned 401. The cookie carried HttpOnly and SameSite=Strict, without Secure on loopback HTTP. The proof script removed its container, volume, network, and cookie jar.
- **Final Linux guest browser proof:** Guest Chromium in the same image opened `http://localhost:5173/`, matching `REMOTECODE_WEB_ORIGIN`. Playwright rejected an incorrect passphrase; valid login reached “Live updates connected” over the authenticated WebSocket; creating action `guest browser 32809827-5a47-447f-860b-8adf4c68af70` produced backend receipt `b25f22d1-4eb3-40eb-9703-dada2c0c6594`; viewport 390×844 had no horizontal overflow. Logout returned to sign-in, and same-origin private `GET /api/actions` returned 401. A host-side Playwright run against the image's published web port passed login, action receipt, and mobile-width verification through Vite's same-origin proxy; direct HTTP access to its separately published API port returned 403 as intended.
- **Environment/security limits:** Secure-cookie behavior was unit-tested against a direct HTTPS request URL, not a live external TLS endpoint. No native Linux host was available; all Linux results are amd64 emulation on macOS arm64. `prototype/start.sh` runs API, web, and Distill without distinct OS identities and passes the startup environment to child processes. This is a confirmed process-environment isolation gap, not a verified agent-side secret-read exploit; RC-015 remains gated on RC-002. Do not run an agent-side exploit or claim secret isolation.
- **Follow-up review fixes — 2026-09-25:** The latest exact-diff review identified two actionable findings. When a valid `local` session logs out, auth now removes every persisted session for that user and closes all authenticated WebSockets, so simultaneous sign-ins cannot leave another tab's private UI active. A 500 ms, non-finite, or otherwise sub-second TTL now returns 503 without a cookie; invalid passwords remain 401. API regression coverage checks 500 ms/no cookie; browser E2E creates two distinct valid session tokens, signs out through the web UI, verifies both old tokens now return 401, and observes private UI cleared in the other tab. `bun run typecheck`, API tests (20 tests/99 expectations), default E2E (4 passed, 2 configuration-dependent skips), expiry E2E at TTL 2500 ms (1 passed), Vite production build, documentation links, Compose validation, shell/Node syntax, and whitespace checks passed.
- **Historical rebuilt Linux proof for these fixes:** At that checkpoint, RC-010 remained unaccepted pending complete review. Image `remotecode-rc010-reviewfix-20260925:local`, manifest `sha256:830759a01b10e055f09bf1ba2bd465728490191269d725363dc413dac69e2c69`, runs Linux amd64 under OrbStack emulation on macOS arm64. Full published-web Playwright against the image passed 4 tests with 2 configuration-dependent skips. Runtime observations: anonymous private route 401; direct published API HTTP login 403/no cookie; supported same-origin web-proxy login 200 and private session/action reads 200; persisted session after removing/recreating the container on the same named volume 200; forced persisted expiry private read 401; logout 204; post-logout private read 401. The test used image manifest digest above and disposable volume `rc010-reviewfix-data-20260925`. A separate Docker restart attempt exited and startup logs showed `Timed out waiting for X11 at :99`; therefore no restart-recovery claim is made. Container recreation with the same volume succeeded. At that historical checkpoint, independent exact-diff review remained pending and RC-010 was unaccepted; the later acceptance decision is recorded above.
- **Latest review finding and correction — 2026-09-25:** The exact-diff reviewer identified that an in-flight sign-in response could authenticate the UI after logout. `signIn` now advances/captures `authEpoch` before the request and ignores stale completions; `signOut` advances the epoch before awaiting the server. New browser regression test creates two persisted sessions, lets the newer login complete, signs out, releases the earlier successful response, and verifies the UI remains at sign-in without private receipts; explicit session checks for both tokens return 401. The next independent review found that assertions followed release without proving delivery of the held response. The test now awaits a signal emitted after `route.fulfill` completes before asserting the UI remains signed out. Focused Playwright passed 1/1 after this correction; the earlier full local E2E passes 5 with 2 configuration-dependent skips; API tests remain 20/99; separate 2500 ms expiry E2E passed 1. At that historical stage, a new exact-diff review was pending; the later clean review and acceptance are recorded above.

Published-web Playwright against this image passed 5 with 2 configuration-dependent skips. With disposable named volume `rc010-reviewfix-final-data-20260925`, runtime checks observed anonymous private route 401, direct API HTTP login 403/no cookie, same-origin login and private access 200, session after container removal/recreation with the same volume 200, forced expiry private read 401, logout 204, and post-logout private route 401. No restart-recovery behavior is claimed; a prior restart attempt on an intermediate candidate timed out waiting for Xvfb at `:99`.
- **Additional review corrections — 2026-09-25:** Review of the previous patch found that the Vite `/api` proxy could carry a non-loopback cleartext login to the API, where the peer appeared loopback; a lost logout response could leave private UI visible; and the delayed-login E2E did not wait for browser response handling. `apps/web/vite.config.ts` now rejects login requests from non-loopback socket peers before proxying. A close-code 4401 revocation now clears private state during a pending logout despite its epoch; the failure branch also clears state if the request eventually errors, while reporting that logout could not be confirmed. The E2E commits logout, holds its HTTP response, confirms WebSocket revocation clears the UI and private route returns 401, and only then aborts the held response. The delayed-login test waits for the browser-observed response, one browser task, and two animation frames before checking sign-in state and no receipt.
- **Proof for these corrections:** The local browser suite passed 7 tests with 2 configuration-dependent skips; non-loopback login E2E passed separately (1), delayed-login and lost-logout-response regressions passed (2), and short-TTL expiry passed separately with `REMOTECODE_AUTH_SESSION_TTL_MS=2500` (1). API suite passed 20 tests/99 expectations, typecheck, build and documentation checks passed. Local host loopback web-proxy login returned 200; login from a non-loopback machine interface and separate Docker peer returned 403 without a cookie. Latest Linux amd64 image `remotecode-rc010-final-20260925:local`, manifest `sha256:a3238a0c2f82f277b7564228fc3c4238b8e247651c776ffe2d1bba8a3771d9bf`, was emulated on macOS ARM. A separate peer received 403 through its web proxy. Guest Chromium logged in locally, created/read action receipt `8a9cdc2a-0d64-4e01-a4e7-7ad7175cedc7`, logged out, and received 401 on the private API route. The proof container was stopped and its test named volume retained. This is not native Linux or live external TLS proof.

## RC-002 — main Distill on Linux

- **Result:** Partially unblocked. Linux Distill v2.0.14 starts in a Debian 12 x86_64 guest under the available Docker/OrbStack emulation, and the authorized cached login reached ACP session setup. RC-002 remains blocked because the real prompt timed out before file verification and the provider effect is unknown; credentials were not inspected.
- **Environment:** macOS host; `distill --version` → `distill 2.0.14 (8e7101188723)`; Docker client/server `29.4.0/29.4.0`; cached `remotecode/computer:local` reports `linux/arm64`.
- **Action:** Ran the cached image with `--network none` and checked `command -v distill` inside it.
- **Observed at the original probe:** `Distill=unavailable`. Local Distill executables were all `macOS-aarch64` Mach-O binaries (versions 2.0.9, 2.0.13, and 2.0.14); `docker images` contained no Distill image. At that time `.sam-harness/config.yaml` set `network: false`, so no runtime or dependency was downloaded and no model was called.
- **ARM64 binary result:** After the user explicitly authorized network use, downloaded the official Distill v2.0.14 `distill-linux-aarch64` release asset; published SHA-256 `abf7e29c56c164476ea81ef6cc6a1b056620b154839c03de3db3ae9a66bb6864` matched. `file` identified an ELF 64-bit ARM aarch64 Linux executable. Mounted it read-only into `remotecode/computer:local` and ran it with networking disabled. The guest reported `aarch64`, glibc 2.36, and resolved dynamic libraries, but `distill --version` terminated with `Illegal instruction` (exit 132).
- **x86_64 runtime follow-up:** The official Distill v2.0.14 Linux x86_64 asset was checksum-verified earlier (SHA-256 `6e21372a858f7b563e038b6f0bdf3dd7da2e8d02f6cc9dfe2e9aca3b269e19a0`). The cached application image is ARM64 and did not support the first `--platform linux/amd64` attempt; executing the x86_64 binary in that ARM64 guest also lacked `/lib64/ld-linux-x86-64.so.2`. To isolate the architecture issue, pulled `debian:12-slim` after network authorization (image manifest digest `sha256:3783cc01769c7b2b1b83a5c5ad96c815348e28ed7da68e2e3687004faa906251`), mounted the binary read-only, and ran with `--network none --platform linux/amd64`. Both `distill --version` (`distill 2.0.14 (8e7101188723)`) and `distill --help` exited 0. Thus a Linux Distill runtime is available in an emulated amd64 Debian guest. The Debian image is only the runtime probe, not the planned RemoteCode guest/image; native target compatibility, model requests, and stdio-backed file-creation/interruption/restart/reconciliation are not established.
- **Authorized model access and stdio attempt:** The user authorized using the existing local Distill account and test profile without sharing secrets. Host `distill models` listed `grok-4.6`; the Linux ACP server offered the non-interactive `cached_token` method. The client received successful `initialize`, `authenticate`, and `session/new` responses with `grok-4.6` selected. It then sent a real `session/prompt` asking Distill to create `rc002-created.txt` in the mounted test workspace. The prompt RPC did not return within 360 seconds; the wrapper terminated the process, and a read-back confirmed the file was absent. The stderr capture was empty. The temporary ACP client only handled permission requests; it did not handle other agent-to-client requests, and it did not retain the incoming protocol transcript. This may explain the timeout, but the exact cause was not captured. The wrapper also failed to preserve the session ID or a usage receipt, so it is unknown whether the provider processed or billed the prompt. Do not retry until that effect is reconciled. No credential values were read or recorded.
- **Remaining proof:** After reconciling the unknown provider effect, fix the stdio test client to handle agent-to-client requests and retain a durable session/usage receipt. Then prove file creation, interruption, backend/process restart, and state/file reconciliation in the target-compatible Linux environment. RC-002 remains blocked; CLI startup, cached-token authentication, and session setup alone do not meet its acceptance criteria.

### RC-002 reconciliation check — 2026-09-25

- **Result:** The artifacts inspected for this check contained no authoritative Distill session or provider usage receipt. An earlier local `distill sessions search 'rc002-created.txt' --limit 5` returned `Total: 0`. That local result does not establish whether the provider processed or billed the prompt. The earlier ACP probe did not retain the `session/new` identifier or protocol transcript, and the prompt wrapper terminated after its 360-second timeout. The local `usage.json` belongs to this Grok harness session; it is not a Distill provider receipt and cannot establish whether the external prompt was processed or billed.
- **Follow-up local search:** Read-only searches for `rc002-created.txt`, `create a file`, `file creation`, and `Linux Distill` each returned one local session. Its ID matches this Grok harness session. Because the ACP `session/new` ID was not retained, the local result cannot be matched to or excluded from that session. The CLI help says session search covers summaries and first prompts, which explains why these terms can match this conversation. This local match is not an authoritative Distill receipt and does not resolve whether the timed-out prompt was processed or billed.
- **Local history lookup:** `distill sessions list --limit 100` exited 0 and showed one unique local session ID, matching the current Grok harness session. The `distill usage` command requires a session ID, and no ACP session ID was retained. This does not provide an authoritative record for the timed-out ACP request.
- **Console route check:** The existing Chrome profile's request to `https://console.x.ai/team/default/logs` redirected to `/welcome?path=/logs` and showed the team-creation onboarding page. No usage or request record was visible; no team was created and no form was submitted.
- **Boundary at that time:** No prompt was resent, no account login was performed, and no team was created. The effect and provider usage remain unknown. This does not satisfy RC-002's file creation, interruption, restart, or reconciliation proof, so RC-002 remains blocked. RC-004 was then gated on RC-002 under the original graph; the later pivot below changes only RC-004's non-executing contract path.
- **Follow-up local-source inventory — 2026-09-25:** Rechecked `distill sessions list --limit 100`, searched `rc002-created.txt`, inspected the retained sanitized source-check artifact, and checked existing Docker container names/status for an RC-002 or Distill test container. The CLI still exposes only the current Grok harness session; the container metadata check found no RC-002 container. No ACP request/session ID or provider usage receipt appeared in these inspected sources, so they cannot resolve the effect. The locally available xAI logs route had already redirected to team onboarding, so no authorized provider log view was available.
- **Captured-session trace check — 2026-09-25:** Searched the saved Grok session's `updates.jsonl`, `chat_history.jsonl`, `events.jsonl`, and captured terminal logs for the ACP attempt and its prompt. The retained ACP client script directory from the original run is absent. The UUIDs found in adjacent matching records are Grok session/prompt metadata or RC-003 action receipts; none is an ACP session identifier or a provider usage receipt. The saved artifacts therefore do not let the ACP request be matched to Distill history.
- **Recovered local request ID — 2026-09-25:** The saved background-task result contains the ACP client's timeout error, `Timed out waiting for Distill request 4`, from `receive_for(4, timeout=360)`. This recovers the client's local request ID, `4`, for the prompt call. The saved result contains no `session/new` response identifier or provider usage receipt. This local request ID does not establish whether Distill processed or billed the prompt and cannot by itself be matched to a provider record.
- **Resume condition:** Proceed only when an authoritative Distill session/usage receipt is available through an already authorized source. Otherwise, keep the uncertain operation unresolved; do not retry it.
- **Pivot policy — 2026-09-25 (historical assessment, superseded for RC-004 sequencing):** Added [`PIVOT.md`](../PIVOT.md) to stop repeated searches of exhausted sources and route future blockers to an equivalent authorized proof, dependency-ready independent work, or safe non-executing preparation. This policy does not weaken RC-002 acceptance, treat the Mac as Linux proof, or permit lifecycle testing while the previous provider effect is unknown. Local session history, saved traces, and the Console route had been checked. At that assessment, no independent task was eligible under the original dependency graph, so the wait condition was an authoritative correlated record. The later pivot permits RC-004's design-only contract; RC-005 is now eligible. RC-002 remains blocked.

## RC-003 — Linux GUI and remote clients

- **Result:** Blocked after a partial Linux/X11 MCP probe; this is not acceptance of the full React Native Web/Elysia/Distill journey.
- **Environment at probe time:** Cached `remotecode/computer:local` image, Debian 12, Linux arm64; `linux-use` source at `fe6b2048f71685672ceeec34a8e765540bc8ba55`, mounted read-only; `.sam-harness/config.yaml` then had `network: false`; Xvfb started on `:99` with `XDG_SESSION_TYPE=x11`. The network authority was explicitly enabled later, but this does not change the observed missing `wmctrl` or unavailable application source.
- **Action:** Sent real JSON-RPC stdio requests (`initialize`, `tools/call` for `doctor`, and `tools/call` for `list_windows`) to the mounted `linux-use/server.py` process in that Linux container.
- **Observed:** Server identified itself as `linux-use` 1.0.0. `doctor` returned `platform=linux`, `display=true`, `session_type=x11`, `xdotool=/usr/bin/xdotool`, `wmctrl=null`, and no supported native tools. `list_windows` returned `isError=true` with `Unsupported/unavailable: required system utility 'wmctrl' is not installed.` No window actions or input were attempted.
- **Known limitations:** `linux-use` documents capture, input, semantic actions, and Chrome automation as unsupported. This planning repository has no `apps/api`, `apps/web`, or container build source; the cached image has no Distill or `wmctrl`. The shared backend/client/browser journey remains unverified.
- **Verification:** `python3 scripts/check-doc-links.py`, RC-003 blocker consistency check, and `git diff --check` passed.

### RC-003 follow-up — Linux amd64 prototype (2026-09-24)

- **Historical result (superseded by the 2026-09-25 proof below):** Partial proof only; cross-browser shared-backend reconciliation was not yet proven.
- **Build/runtime:** Built `remotecode-rc003:local` for `linux/amd64` with the pinned Distill v2.0.14 binary checksum and `linux-use` revision `fe6b2048f71685672ceeec34a8e765540bc8ba55`. The isolated guest started Xvfb, Openbox, Elysia, Vite/React Native Web, and Chromium. Ports were loopback-published on the macOS host.

No model prompt or credential use occurred.
- **Browser/API:** `bun run test:e2e` passed the local external-browser action receipt and mobile-width test (1 passed, the guest-only test skipped). Against the live guest (`RC003_WEB_URL=http://127.0.0.1:35173`, API `http://127.0.0.1:33100`), the external-browser action/receipt test passed. Typecheck, API tests (3 tests/7 expectations), and Vite production build passed. An initial React render defect (Eden supplies a `Date` for `createdAt`) was fixed by rendering its string representation.

The CDP design was revised so Playwright runs inside the guest via `docker exec`, and the final proof below completes the journey while keeping CDP loopback-only. - **Historical commands:** `docker build --platform linux/amd64 -f prototype/Dockerfile -t remotecode-rc003:local .`; `RC003_WEB_URL=http://127.0.0.1:35173 RC003_API_URL=http://127.0.0.1:33100 bun run test:e2e -- --grep "external browser"`. ### RC-003 final proof — same-backend guest/external browser exchange (2026-09-25)

- **Result:** RC-003 accepted against its current prototype acceptance contract. This proves the X11/API/web/MCP demonstration only; it does not prove Distill prompt execution on Linux (RC-002), persistent product hosting, security isolation, or unsupported capture/input capabilities.
- **Runtime:** Docker container `rc003-linux-proof-final`, image `remotecode-rc003:local`, `linux/amd64`. Host bindings were `127.0.0.1:33100 -> guest:3000` and `127.0.0.1:35173 -> guest:5173`; port 9222 was not published. The rebuilt Chromium command uses `--remote-debugging-address=127.0.0.1`. Inside the guest, `/proc/net/tcp` showed the port 9222 listener bound to `0100007F:2406`, and `http://127.0.0.1:9222/json/version` responded. A second container on the default bridge attempted to connect to the guest container IP at port 9222 and got `connect_ex: 111` (connection refused). After the guest Playwright helper disconnected, Chromium stayed running, its window remained listed by `wmctrl`, and the loopback CDP endpoint still responded.
- **Real browser roundtrip:** On the corrected image, command `RC003_GUEST_CONTAINER=rc003-linux-proof-final RC003_WEB_URL=http://127.0.0.1:35173 RC003_API_URL=http://127.0.0.1:33100 bun run test:e2e` passed all three E2E tests. The external browser created receipt `4b7382a1-7751-470c-9bcf-3a21953028e7` for action `external 577185e1-b5b9-4863-9f11-176913dc9b6f`; guest Chromium observed the same receipt ID. Guest Chromium then created receipt `59cbf5c6-7cd9-4124-9258-c208fcfefd5a` for action `linux guest c74698bf-a486-4727-bd7c-52aa6d94a81e`; the external browser observed that ID. A direct `GET /api/actions` from the host returned both records and IDs. The E2E generates new UUID-backed action descriptions each run and asserts IDs/content against backend history. The full output is preserved by the active goal session; rerun the command above to reproduce.
- **Fail-closed behavior on corrected image:** `docker exec rc003-linux-proof-final python3 scripts/linux-use-smoke.py` returned `proof_window_found=true`, Linux/X11, one window, and `unsupported_confirmed=["screenshot","left_click","type"]`. `docker exec rc003-linux-proof-final distill mcp doctor linux-use --json` reported a healthy stdio handshake, protocol `2024-11-05`, and 15 discovered tools. Each unsupported operation was invoked only after doctor reported it unsupported; the smoke verified an explicit unsupported/unavailable error and made no input action.
- **Automated checks on the corrected build:** `bun run typecheck` passed; `bun test apps/api/src/app.test.ts` passed 3 tests/7 expectations; `bunx vite build --config apps/web/vite.config.ts` passed; default `bun run test:e2e` passed 2 tests and skipped the guest-only test without guest settings; guest-configured full E2E passed all 3 tests. `python3 scripts/check-doc-links.py`, `python3 -m py_compile scripts/linux-use-smoke.py`, `sh -n scripts/start-linux-prototype.sh prototype/start.sh`, `node --check scripts/linux-guest-browser-proof.mjs`, and `git diff --check` passed. An earlier attempted nonexistent script name `bun run test:api` failed with “Script not found”; the defined API test command passed instead.
- **Review finding resolved:** Independent review found the previous launch setting used `0.0.0.0` and this evidence section was missing. The launch now sets `127.0.0.1`; the image was rebuilt, guest and external E2E rerun, listener binding inspected, a peer container's connection refused, and Chromium/CDP/window persistence checked after helper disconnect. This section records the corrected results; request a fresh review of the updated diff.
- **Initial next gate (before the plan pivot):** RC-004 was gated on RC-002 under the original dependency graph. Never retry the uncertain prompt absent authoritative reconciliation. This assessment was superseded by the later design-only RC-004 pivot; the guest container is owned by that earlier run and should not be touched unless still present and owned by the current run.

### Plan pivot and RC-004 contract — 2026-09-25

It can be completed from the approved inventory and documented product flows without assuming what happened in the timed-out ACP request.

The duplicate case is injected after durable acceptance but before Distill starts; client-drop is after durable success; host restart is before a completion receipt. Each has one allowed outcome and a recovery rule.
- **Acceptance boundary:** RC-004 can be accepted only as this design contract. RC-002's Linux file creation, interruption, restart, and state/file reconciliation remain unverified. No provider prompt was sent during this pivot.

## RC-006 — Elysia lifecycle and dependency readiness — 2026-09-25

- **Result:** Accepted for the task's API lifecycle and health-check contract. This is Linux amd64 emulation on the macOS arm64 Docker host, not native amd64. It does not establish the persistence requirements of RC-008 or run Distill.
- **Essential dependency at this stage:** The API's configured SQLite database. Distill is not an API readiness dependency; its execution and supervision remain gated by RC-002. Actions are currently in-memory and are not claimed durable.
- **Implementation:** `apps/api/src/app.ts` configures `DATABASE_PATH` (default `/tmp/remotecode.sqlite`), initializes the SQLite file when possible, and gives health routes a read-only query against the essential `actions` table. `/api/health/ready` has a 500 ms route deadline; the SQLite query runs in a Worker terminated after 400 ms so a blocking synchronous SQLite call cannot stall the API event loop. A readiness failure returns HTTP 503; `/api/health/live` reports process liveness independently. The prototype startup waits for readiness. Web/mobile typed callers use the readiness route and include `not_ready` in the response type.
- **Prior attempt, not accepted:** The initial synchronous SQLite check passed 7 tests/20 expectations. Its earlier image, `sha256:a52e52ed9943719bfebe3298711139afe71e9d37c3a15ad7de982b2799e80724`, returned 503 after a 67 ms lock probe. Independent review correctly identified that this did not prove a blocking synchronous query could be interrupted; readiness was not accepted on that evidence. The probe was moved to a terminable Worker and acceptance was deferred until the final proof below.
- **Final local checks:** `bun run typecheck` passed. `bun test apps/api/src/app.test.ts` passed 7 tests and 23 expectations. One test holds SQLite `BEGIN EXCLUSIVE`, observes the API fail readiness before 500 ms with liveness still available, then uses a separate read-only query with a 550 ms busy timeout to verify the same lock continues to block SQLite for at least 500 ms. Readiness recovers after unlock. A separate never-resolving asynchronous check exercised the route's 500 ms fallback (observed about 503 ms including timer scheduling). `python3 scripts/check-doc-links.py` and `git diff --check` passed.
- **Final Linux image:** Built with `docker build --platform linux/amd64 -f prototype/Dockerfile -t remotecode-rc006-api:local .`. Image manifest list digest: `sha256:39c94314d77e7e3260dd165933015ee75c419cb424f18f18bbd850e6ef0bd415`; platform: `linux/amd64` emulated on the arm64 host.
- **Lifecycle and failure proof:** Started the API as the container's foreground Bun process using `docker run -d --platform linux/amd64 --name remotecode-rc006-linux-worker-proof-20260925 --entrypoint bun remotecode-rc006-api:local apps/api/src/index.ts`. Readiness returned HTTP 200. Inside the container, a separate Python SQLite connection set a 5,000 ms busy timeout, held `BEGIN EXCLUSIVE`, and remained alive; API readiness returned HTTP 503 in 0.417293 seconds while concurrent liveness returned HTTP 200 in 0.009226 seconds. After terminating the lock holder, readiness returned 200. `docker stop -t 5` reported `exited`; `docker start` restored readiness to 200; the container was stopped and removed afterward.
- **Failure condition checked:** A real SQLite query remained blocked beyond the readiness deadline, but the Worker-based readiness request returned within the 500 ms contract and did not block liveness. No production database, volume durability, or data recovery behavior is inferred; those remain RC-008.
- **Next eligible work:** RC-008, “Persist data outside the image,” can proceed from RC-006. RC-007 and all live Distill execution remain gated on RC-002; its provider effect is still unknown and no prompt was repeated.

## RC-013 shared Eden connection accepted 2026-09-26

- **Decision:** RC-013 is accepted against its original Delivery / end, Executable proof, and Failure if criteria after the shared API call was exercised through web and a minimal native iOS app, a route-field mutation was detected by typechecking, and the corrected complete change received independent reasoning review. The acceptance criteria were not reduced. Reviewer `01a0dc96-f386-7b90-8ece-a30a63f190b4` found no blocking defect and confirmed the root monorepo typecheck is the relevant shared-contract check for both consumers; the review was source/evidence limited and did not itself rerun tests or inspect Git. The final local check commands and their outputs are recorded below.
- **Eligibility:** RC-005, RC-006, and RC-010 are accepted in the task record. RC-013's start condition (exported Elysia API type) is met. RC-013 does not require live Distill execution; RC-002 remains unknown, unaccepted, and untouched.
- **Implementation:** One shared typed Eden client backs web/mobile health calls, credentials, caller headers, a 10-second deadline, cancellation propagation, and typed unknown-outcome handling. Its deadline uses `AbortController` plus a timer instead of `AbortSignal.timeout/any`, which the native runtime does not provide. Unknown outcomes are returned through `new Response(...)`, rather than `Response.json`, which is absent from the installed React Native fetch implementation. The web actions screen and a minimal Expo app display health state and expose refresh controls.
- **Direct checks:** Real Elysia integration invokes the web and mobile service modules and confirms both return `ready`; authenticated cookie forwarding succeeds and anonymous private session access returns 401. Client coverage verifies absent AbortSignal static helpers; timeout before headers, in JSON/text bodies, and while reading a stream; active and already-aborted caller cancellation; and timeout normalization with `Response.json` removed. Independent web-only and mobile-only TypeScript project checks both pass from the repository compiler settings. A temporary API contract mutation changing both ready-route response branches from `status` to `state` caused each consumer-only typecheck to fail in the shared client's typed response read; the API source was restored byte-for-byte. An earlier one-branch mutation did not fail because the response union retained a `status` field in its other branch; it is not used as acceptance evidence.
- **User-visible behavior:** The browser health journey verified ready → request unavailable → ready by clicking the page refresh control; the full E2E suite passed 8 tests and skipped 3 configuration-dependent cases. A Release iOS simulator app (`com.remotecode.mobileproof`, iOS 18.5) was rebuilt after the final client fix, installed with an embedded `main.jsbundle` (SHA-256 `633f49bbf688bbe2a0263eaad9ceb22716c8643c3458b4e743c82f1cc0930aaa`), and called the real local Elysia API. Screenshots in `{SCRATCH}` show ready, unavailable after stopping the API, and ready after restarting it. This proves the app call and recovery states, not Android or deployment.
- **Verification:** On final source, `bun run typecheck`; `bun test packages/client/src/index.test.ts` (9 tests/30 expectations); `bun test apps/api/src` (20/99); `bun run test:e2e -- --workers=1` (8 passed/3 skipped); `bunx vite build --config apps/web/vite.config.ts`; `python3 scripts/check-doc-links.py`; and `git diff --check` passed. Captured logs and three simulator screenshots are under `{SCRATCH}` (`rc013-broken-contract-both-platforms.log`, `rc013-mobile-response-fix-ready.png`, `rc013-mobile-response-fix-unavailable.png`, `rc013-mobile-response-fix-recovery.png`, plus terminal output files in the goal session).

Replaced it with standard `new Response(...)`, added a test that removes the static helper and exercises a real timeout, rebuilt the Release simulator app, and repeated the real API state journey. The follow-up review found no blocking defect. Its stated limits: it had no shell/Git tool and did not independently rerun checks; the native screenshots do not demonstrate native timeout classification or authenticated session behavior.

Native timeout classification, native authenticated-session behavior, native refresh-button input, Android, deployment, and unresolved UX decisions from `sam-harness plan` were not verified. ## RC-014 action-event recovery slice — partial, not accepted — 2026-09-26

- **Acceptance boundary:** Direct prerequisites RC-004, RC-008, and RC-013 are recorded accepted. PR #2's published RC-013 head `aba20f5996b764563b33424df8c79e1fa90b1101` was rechecked with `gh pr view 2 --json headRefOid,state,statusCheckRollup`; it is open at that SHA and both `static` and `test` checks succeeded in each of runs `36228061714` and `36228060027`. RC-002's prior Distill provider effect remains unknown and was not investigated, retried, or used. RC-014 is **not accepted**: existing action receipts are synchronous mutations, not supervised runs, and no real run executor exists to exercise the task's “disconnect during run” proof.
- **Implementation:** `actions.sequence INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT` now provides a durable monotonic cursor. Initialization transactionally migrates the previous text-primary-key schema, preserving stored receipt IDs, descriptions, timestamps, and deterministic order. Snapshot and `action.created` messages carry cursors; snapshots return action history and its high-water cursor from the same synchronous database read. Authenticated `sync` requests re-read the snapshot. HTTP receipt responses remain unchanged. A failed event send no longer turns a committed receipt into an apparent failed write.
- **Shared-client and web behavior:** `packages/client/src/action-events.ts` validates event envelopes, replaces history from authoritative snapshots, applies only the next cursor, ignores duplicates/old cursors, and requests one snapshot on a gap. The web client removed the competing startup GET merge and waits for a valid snapshot before declaring the socket connected. It clears private UI on socket loss, requires an authenticated session check before manual reconnect, times out stalled synchronization after five seconds, rejects stale socket/HTTP callbacks, and does not resend POST during recovery. Existing logout/expiry behavior remains enforced. `plan/mockups.html`, `.sam-harness/config.yaml`, and `.sam-harness/UX_GATES.md` now describe the recovery interaction and its source contract. `sam-harness plan` was rerun; the output still lists unresolved overall adoption/design decisions, and no Harness plan was applied or UX approval claimed.
- **API/shared-client proof:** `bun run test` runs the configured API and client tests: 34 tests / 148 expectations passed. API coverage includes authenticated real WebSocket snapshot/cursor and `sync` recovery plus a legacy SQLite fixture migrated and initialized again with original and next sequence values preserved. Shared reducer tests cover authoritative replacement, sequential delivery, duplicate suppression, single repair request after a gap, snapshot recovery, and malformed input.
- **Browser proof:** `bun run test:e2e` passed 9 tests; 3 configuration-dependent tests were skipped. New Playwright tests used the real API/WebSocket through `routeWebSocket`: held a browser POST response after durable commit, suppressed its live event, disconnected and verified immediate private-state clearing, released the single response, explicitly reconnected, and observed the exact backend receipt in the authoritative snapshot. A duplicate was injected and history count stayed unchanged; exactly one POST was observed. Another test dropped an intermediate event, forwarded the next cursor, and observed snapshot gap repair. The reconnect control was checked at 390×844 with no horizontal overflow. A separate `REMOTECODE_AUTH_SESSION_TTL_MS=2500` browser run passed the disconnect/expiry/reconnect-authentication regression (1 test). Existing cross-tab logout and delayed-write/logout checks passed. The obsolete late-startup-GET test was removed because startup state now comes from the ordered WebSocket snapshot; its source of truth and relevant races are covered by reconnect and gap E2E.
- **Local commands:** `bun run typecheck`; `bun run test`; `bunx vite build --config apps/web/vite.config.ts`; `python3 scripts/check-doc-links.py`; `git diff --check`; `sam-harness pipeline . --phase static --receipt true`; full `bun run test:e2e`; and short-TTL E2E as stated above. Output files are saved in the goal scratch directory as `rc014-typecheck.log`, `rc014-unit-api-client-tests.log`, `rc014-vite-build.log`, `rc014-doc-links.log`, `rc014-diff-check.log`, `rc014-harness-static.log`, `rc014-e2e-full.log`, and `rc014-e2e-expiry.log`. This is macOS local verification; no native Linux, deployment, or production proof is claimed.
- **First full-suite findings:** The initial E2E pass exposed assertions that expected one visible label even though the accepted UI intentionally renders the receipt in both “latest” and history, plus a startup-GET race test whose premise no longer exists after adopting a single ordered snapshot channel. Updated the two duplicate-view expectations and removed that obsolete request-path test; the next full run passed. No product failure was observed in these corrected assertions.
- **Reasoning review and corrections — 2026-09-26:** Reviewer `01a0dcd8-ec92-7f22-ad91-bf560bf7ebe6` found two P2 web defects: malformed-event recovery called browser-forbidden close code 1002 without immediately clearing receipts/disabling writes; a reconnect session check invalidated by sign-out could leave reconnect progress stuck. Both malformed-message paths now clear private state, disable writes, expose reconnect and close with application code 4002; sign-out and session revocation reset reconnect progress while stale-response guards remain. Added malformed-JSON browser E2E; focused test passed. The next corrected-patch review `01a0dcdc-faa7-7c83-84fd-7bbd03ccbf42` found readiness could report healthy after legacy action cursor migration failed at startup. The readiness worker now validates the required `sequence` column. Regression fixture creates all required domain/auth tables and indexes before locking, leaving only legacy `actions`; targeted test passed. Mutation replacing cursor validation with generic table validation caused the test to fail as expected (readiness 200 instead of expected 503); source was restored and targeted test passed again. Full current-tree verification after the readiness logic change: `bun run typecheck`; `bun run test` (35 tests/150 expectations); Vite production build; `bun run test:e2e` (10 passed, 3 configuration-dependent skips); docs-link check; `git diff --check`; `sam-harness pipeline . --phase static --receipt true` (passed; receipt `.sam-harness/evidence/20260926T084445.218694000Z-pipeline-static.json`). Final source-level reasoning review `01a0dce1-32b5-73b0-b96b-a6e3990fe1b4` found no concrete defect in reviewed code/evidence and confirmed partial RC-014/unknown RC-002 boundaries. The reviewer could not access Git/shell and therefore did not independently certify the complete base-to-working-tree diff or rerun tests; local Git diff and test evidence were checked separately in this session. ## RC-012 — existing persisted-resource ownership slice — partial, not accepted — 2026-09-26

- **Eligibility and scope:** RC-004 and RC-010 are accepted. The accepted persisted SQLite resource layer exists for workspaces, profiles, and workspace history. RC-012's overall delivery still includes Bot, thread, file, and task routes that do not exist, plus a complete multi-account user journey not provided by the current single-identity password login; therefore this is an implementation checkpoint, not task acceptance.
- **Implementation:** Existing workspace list/create, profile read/update, and history read/create now derive `userId` from the validated persisted request session and scope SQL by owner. History reads return 404 for a workspace owned by another user, while an owned workspace with no history returns 200 and an empty list. Logout deletes only sessions for the validated session's user and closes only that user's active event sockets.
- **Behavior proof:** Added API integration tests using two distinct persisted session identities. The tests verify workspace/profile/history isolation, no cross-user history write, cross-owner history read 404 versus owned-empty 200, and logout of A while B remains authenticated and can request a new WebSocket snapshot. A separate anonymous-path test exercises workspace GET/POST, profile GET/PUT, and history GET/POST; all return 401 and persisted workspace/profile/history counts remain zero. The sessions are seeded directly; this does not prove account creation/login or gateway routing for two real accounts.
- **Review:** First source review found two P2 issues: cross-owner history GET returned indistinguishable empty success, and anonymous storage routes were untested. Corrected history GET to establish workspace ownership and return 404, retained 200 for owned empty history, and added unauthenticated route and persistence assertions. Corrected-patch reviewer `01a0dd30-43a2-7161-a64e-35db9cafc753` found no concrete finding in the inspected source and tests. The reviewer had no shell/Git execution, so could not independently certify the complete diff or rerun commands; the review does not cover missing resource routes or a real multi-account login/gateway journey.

## RC-068 — existing persisted-resource ownership proof accepted — 2026-09-26

- **Decision:** RC-068 is complete for the existing workspace, profile, and workspace-history routes. No Bot, thread, file, workspace-task, or Bot-routine route was present or claimed. RC-012 remains partial and unaccepted; its original criteria are unchanged.

The owner-isolation test exercises A and B workspace list/create, profile read/update, and history read/create. B receives the same HTTP status and body for foreign and nonexistent workspace IDs on both history GET and POST. Foreign writes return 404 and a full readback of history rows before/after is identical. Requests with `userId: "user-b"` under A's session cannot change workspace/history/profile ownership; durable `user_id` values remain `user-a`. Anonymous workspace/profile/history reads and writes return 401, and row counts remain zero.
- **Focused verification:** `bun test apps/api/src/app.test.ts -t "isolates workspace, history, and profile access by persisted session identity"` passed with 38 expectations. Full API output is captured at `{SCRATCH}/rc068-api-final.log`.

## RC-016 dependency pivot — reviewed planning change — 2026-09-26

- **Source and gates:** RC-013 is accepted, providing the typed Elysia API and shared Eden client. RC-014's task contract requires durable cursor/snapshot/gap recovery and ultimately a disconnect-during-real-run executable proof. Its current implementation has a proven action-event slice but no run executor; the run criterion is still unverified and RC-014 remains partial. RC-002 remains unknown/unaccepted, and is not changed or investigated by this pivot.
- **Amendment:** In `plan/tasks.html`, RC-016's sole dependency is now RC-013. RC-014 task acceptance is no longer a prerequisite because RC-016's original Start is typed API/shared Eden, and a version/capabilities endpoint, compatibility checks, and server-side input validation can be implemented without a real Distill executor. RC-016's original Start, Delivery, Executable proof, and Failure if text remain unchanged. No task status is promoted by this planning edit.
- **Required proof qualification:** The RC-016 proof must run identified previous and current client versions against the current server. Because the installed web client independently consumes native WebSocket snapshots/events and reducer semantics, endpoint-only/changed-header tests are insufficient. A supported version must complete the actual client journey through compatible snapshot/event handling and persisted mutation/readback; an incompatible version must show an actionable correction message before any durable write. Verify database state directly. State the same supported-version policy for hosted and self-managed modes without claiming either deployment exists.
- **Reasoning review:** Reviewer `01a0df09-a57b-7451-b11d-daa5fde1f4a7` approved removal of full RC-014 acceptance as a dependency, based on RC-013 and the independent Elysia/SQLite HTTP validation boundary. It found a material coupling: supported compatibility proof cannot exclude the current event/snapshot interface, and merely changing a header on the current client is not a previous-client proof. The task note preserves both requirements. The reviewer did not edit files, run tests, or independently verify receipts.
- **Final plan review and parent checks:** Exact-patch reasoning reviewer `01a0df0d-00f2-70b2-a54c-254552edd0ad` found no concrete defect; it confirmed the dependency rationale, unchanged RC-016 core contract, event/snapshot compatibility obligation, and preserved acceptance/deployment boundaries. Parent checks passed `python3 scripts/check-doc-links.py`, `git diff --check`, a core-field comparison against pre-amendment `HEAD`, and graph assertions for unique IDs and valid dependency anchors. Review was read-only; it did not execute those commands or certify runtime behavior.
- **Boundaries and next step:** RC-014 remains In progress with its original acceptance and real-run failure criterion unchanged; no fake executor or replacement run was added. RC-011 remains `REVISE` with no council continuation. This is a planning-only dependency change, not RC-016 acceptance. The reviewed sequencing amendment authorizes the next planned slice: implement RC-016's compatibility scope, including identified previous/current client versions, event/snapshot interface proof, actionable pre-write rejection, and explicit policy for hosted/self-managed modes. Preserve the still-open RC-014 criterion.

## RC-016 compatibility implementation checkpoint — partial, not accepted — 2026-09-26

- **Implementation:** Added `/api/version` capability data and explicit client policy (`CLIENT_VERSION = 1`; supported client range 0–1 for both hosted and self-managed policy entries). Unsupported HTTP clients receive 426 before feature handlers can write, and unsupported event sockets close with 4406 before any snapshot. Action payload validation remains server-side. - **State and failure proof:** Elysia/SQLite integration verified current and legacy writes persist once, unsupported-version and malformed writes add no durable records, and incompatible WebSocket clients receive no snapshot. Browser coverage verified actionable 426/4406 guidance; startup and reconnect 426 paths keep the user out of private state, clear a prior receipt on disconnect, and do not issue another write. ## Native-client route and backlog eligibility reassessment — 2026-09-26

- **Initial finding:** The Expo app in `apps/mobile/App.tsx` called only `getMobileHealth`, which delegates to `/api/health/ready`; that route is intentionally outside version rejection and does not authenticate, create/read action receipts, or receive event snapshots. This path could not close the native/installed-client proof gap.
- **Scope correction:** The initial conclusion that a shipped mobile flow necessarily exceeded RC-016 was too restrictive. A minimal app flow that signs into the existing host, submits one action through the existing API, receives the existing authenticated snapshot/live event stream, and displays confirmed receipts directly exercises RC-016's current-client compatibility criterion. It does not implement the later workspace/thread/takeover journey. Use existing endpoints only; no test-only product behavior, new backend route, token exposure, cleartext exception, or origin-validation relaxation.
- **Proof plan:** Build/install the actual Expo app in an iOS simulator, connect it to the local server using the existing HTTP cookie and WebSocket authorization contracts, verify snapshot plus confirmed action receipt, and read that receipt back from backend state. Use the existing frozen prior-browser artifact and incompatible-version regression path for legacy/rejection evidence; do not replace that artifact with current code. Record simulator bundle identity and API revision. If native platform cookie/origin behavior cannot pass without weakening security, retain it as unverified and diagnose rather than bypassing safeguards.
- **Policy and eligibility:** The RC-016 delivery requires explicit hosted/self-managed supported-version policy; it does not require a production deployment. Actual deployment remains unverified and unauthorized (`deploy: false`). Recorded backlog: 68 tasks, 9 complete, 3 in progress (RC-012/014/016), 1 blocked (RC-002), 55 to do. RC-017 depends on RC-016 acceptance; RC-018/020/021 remain downstream. RC-012/014 still have incomplete delivery/proof; RC-011 still lacks routing and has a `REVISE` design. The mobile slice is the concrete eligible continuation; do not retry RC-002 or change RC-011 boundaries.

## RC-016 native implementation and local proof checkpoint — 2026-09-26 — partial, not accepted

- **Product change:** `apps/mobile/App.tsx` now provides host password sign-in/session validation through the shared Eden client; an authenticated WebSocket sends `CLIENT_VERSION` in its query and the exact configured `Origin` header; snapshot/event messages use the shared reducer and request snapshot repair on cursor gaps; authenticated action submission shows the server-returned receipt; compatibility errors direct the user to update the host or use a supported client. No token/cookie is surfaced, and no endpoint or server security policy changed. `plan/mockups.html` documents the corresponding mobile state and incompatibility guidance.
- **Targeted checks:** `bun run typecheck` passed. `bun run test` passed 41 API/client tests / 217 assertions. Initial project-only `bunx tsc --noEmit -p apps/mobile/tsconfig.json` failed because Expo's project config did not include Bun API typings; the authoritative configured monorepo typecheck passed. `sam-harness plan . --profile baseline --output {SCRATCH}/mobile-plan.json` ran after the design update and reported unresolved design/authority choices with no repository changes planned; no plan approval is claimed. The app Metro entry bundle returned HTTP 200 and contained the configured host/origin and sign-in/action/receipt labels.
- **Real local API behavior:** API source revision is the current uncommitted tree based on `14f8ab0267cbba26871e117bc9d779ea1698c2e2`; server used Bun 1.3.13 on macOS, isolated SQLite `{SCRATCH}/mobile-proof.sqlite`, API `http://127.0.0.1:3000`, password configured only in the process environment, and allowed Origin `http://localhost:5173`. `/api/version` returned API 1 and hosted/self-managed client range 0–1. A current-version authenticated action returned receipt `2a0b871b-f975-4fc3-8e3a-31770bc8f530`; a distinct authenticated history read returned the exact same receipt ID/action/timestamp. An unsupported HTTP version returned 426 with actionable guidance; SQLite action count stayed 0 before and after that rejected write. Captured sanitized outputs are `{SCRATCH}/action-receipt.json`, `{SCRATCH}/action-history.json`, and `{SCRATCH}/incompatible.json`.
- **Native build and gap:** `cd apps/mobile && EXPO_PUBLIC_API_ORIGIN=http://127.0.0.1:3000 EXPO_PUBLIC_CLIENT_ORIGIN=http://localhost:5173 bunx expo run:ios --device 2298C16C-9DE2-40A8-9630-23144257CB4C` built and installed bundle `com.remotecode.mobileproof` on the already-booted iPhone 16 Pro simulator (iOS 18.5). Expo initially found unrelated `lacco-app` on port 8081; it was left untouched. The project Metro server on port 8082 produced `apps/mobile/index` with expected UI/env values; its debug client initially showed a stale `ExpoLocalization` error from the other bundle. Pivoted to the actual Release simulator build with `--configuration Release --port 8082`, which completed, installed, and visibly rendered the sign-in screen; screenshot is `{SCRATCH}/mobile-screen-release.png`. The provided accessibility workflow could not interact because `idb` is absent; mac-use window enumeration timed out. Thus native sign-in submission, cookie-jar use, Origin acceptance, native snapshot/event, native action receipt, and native incompatibility are still **unverified**. No changes were made to another app/server or transport policy.
- **Decision:** This implementation/build is a partial checkpoint only, not RC-016 acceptance. Keep the actual supported native app journey outstanding; existing frozen prior-browser plus live-API 426 evidence still supplies the browser-side legacy/current and pre-write cases, but is not a substitute for the native current-client criterion. Do not advance RC-017. RC-016 hosted/self-managed deployment proof remains unverified and unauthorized; RC-014, RC-002 and RC-011 retain their existing status/boundaries. `scripts/__pycache__/` remains unstaged.
- **Independent review correction:** Reasoning reviewer `01a0e074-a1fe-72c1-a441-78c521423815` found four supported defects: Eden may revive an ISO timestamp to `Date` before React rendering; logout ignored returned/throwing errors and cleared visible state only after network completion; a cursor gap could remain shown as connected and permit writes; and socket setup had no snapshot deadline. Corrected mobile handling in `apps/mobile/App.tsx`: normalize POST receipt timestamps to ISO strings, invalidate delayed operations and immediately clear local private state on logout while surfacing uncertain host revocation, only enable connected/write behavior after an authoritative snapshot, request one gap repair with a five-second deadline, close and clear on malformed input, and expose retry/sign-in after transport failures. The reviewer did not inspect a proposed patch or run commands; these corrections are parent-authored. Follow-up source review `01a0e07e-b827-79b0-922d-a6f7721d8ed8` found two more P2 issues: logout was unavailable on a dropped event stream, and close 4401 did not immediately clear private UI/password or invalidate pending operations. It also found a stale build-status handoff. Corrected logout visibility for all non-signed-out states, cleared/invalidate on 4401, and updated the chronology. Final source checks and Release build are recorded below; the exact patch is frozen separately for review. Review of that frozen patch `01a0e084-62af-7a91-8dc3-c47e872311ac` found two P2 session-control defects: a failed reconnect after an authenticated connection could hide logout while retaining old private state, and sign-out could race an in-flight login that creates a session after the logout request. It also reviewed before latest correction and could not verify the provided patch hash, execute checks, or reproduce native behavior. Corrected failure cleanup so every failed login/session attempt clears receipt/action state and remains in a recoverable disconnected state with logout available, because an older cookie or just-created session may still be active; sign-out is disabled only while login/session HTTP calls are outstanding, then re-enabled when they settle. After this correction, root typecheck/API-client tests passed again (41 tests / 217 assertions), and the Release build installed/launched successfully with zero errors/warnings. Final embedded bundle SHA-256 is `6e1f92843bd380b0f0a60a542e01defd41bd9132216ad4cde55933aecf7e6176`; screenshot `{SCRATCH}/mobile-screen-final-release-2.png` shows only the signed-out screen. This does not prove native interaction. A fresh complete-patch review is required.
- **Post-correction checks:** `bun run typecheck && bun run test` passed on the corrected source: 41 tests / 217 assertions. The iOS simulator health check passed seven checks, while the provided accessibility mapper failed with `FileNotFoundError: idb`, consistent with `idb` being unavailable. No alternate safe semantic interaction route is available; the earlier mac-use window enumeration timed out, and it was not repeated. A fresh `cd apps/mobile && EXPO_PUBLIC_API_ORIGIN=http://127.0.0.1:3000 EXPO_PUBLIC_CLIENT_ORIGIN=http://localhost:5173 bunx expo run:ios --configuration Release --port 8082 --device 2298C16C-9DE2-40A8-9630-23144257CB4C` completed successfully after the 4401/logout-control correction but before the later failed-reconnect/login-order correction (Metro bundled 584 modules; Xcode Build Succeeded, 0 errors/warnings; app installed/launched as `com.remotecode.mobileproof`). Intermediate embedded bundle SHA-256: `721d99438ab9fd483aeafa7f8bd94a7650d37b4f95e33c7168bcbc1daee1903f`. `xcrun simctl io ... screenshot` saved `{SCRATCH}/mobile-screen-final-release.png`; it shows the signed-out password screen. This is build/render evidence only and does not prove any tap or authenticated behavior.
- **Acceptance boundary:** No native sign-in, cookie propagation, Origin acceptance, WebSocket snapshot/gap recovery, displayed POST receipt, logout revocation, or installed incompatibility flow was exercised. Native UX criterion therefore remains unverified; RC-016 stays partial/unaccepted and RC-017 remains gated. Hosted/self-managed deployed behavior is unverified and deployment remains unauthorized. RC-002 is unknown/unaccepted and untouched; RC-014's real-run disconnect proof remains open; RC-011 remains `REVISE`. Do not publish or claim native acceptance on build/render proof alone. `scripts/__pycache__/` remains unstaged.
- **Next:** Freeze and review the exact current source+handoff diff, resolve any finding and rerun focused checks, then run final documentation/whitespace checks and publish the scoped partial checkpoint only after review. Keep native behavior explicitly unverified unless a safe, supported interaction route becomes available.
